Scheduler v5 抽样审计·第四轮 — 随机 50 局，首次由厨师给 assistant 打分 (v5 sample audit, round 4)

生成于 2026-09-12 23:09 UTC · 50 个 transcript 由 auditor（每个 assistant 一个）逐字读完 · 全部 50 个 episode 的机械扫描 · 文中引用的每个 transcript 都可点开

0 · 这一轮是什么、怎么读

审计对象。 Scheduler v5 抽样第四轮：随机抽取的 50 个 episode。抽样 seed 1190331951（tmp/assist_bench_v5_sample4/plan.json）：suite 里 50 个 case 每个恰好出现一次；persona 这一轮均衡分配（classic_novice 18 / baseline 16 / classic_expert 16，不再逐局随机 —— 因为现在是厨师给 assistant 打分，而厨师是透过自己的 trust level 打分的，persona 偏了整轮数字就偏了）；assistant 在 6 个 API 模型里均衡随机（每个 8–9 局）；和前三轮没有任何一个 (case, persona, assistant) 三元组重复。frames 观测，tight pace，latency 实测计费（上限 30 tick）。全部 50 局跑在同一个 engine stamp 3bcd378dec 上。

这一轮之前改了什么（三件大事）。

  1. 厨师给 assistant 打分（COOKSIM_GRADER=cook，2026-09-12 起为默认）。engine 不再把判定喂给厨师；厨师被告知自己正在犯的错（commission 类错误直接点名，attention 类不点名、改成「被指控时先看自己的 state map」），每回合在私有字段 assistant_error_call 里回答 none / correct / wrong。它的 "correct" 负责 arm，它的 accept 仍然负责 release —— 认账和照做是两件事。detection credit 现在是并集：flagged_valid = 厨师说 correct 或 engine 的 flag verdict 正确。
  2. 一份 kitchen rulebook，两边共读：从引擎生成（station 表、时间、容量、这张地图实际有的 tile），厨师读第二人称版本，assistant 拿到的是「厨师读到的那一份」的原文引用。
  3. 食物渲染按品类和状态重画（之前所有 cooked 物品都是同一个原语染成 80% 棕色），另外 serving pass 旁边那个「服务员」去掉了 —— 它在画面里读起来像第二个厨师。side_quest 这一族已按你的裁定删除。

做了什么。 50 局机械扫描（Appendix A）；6 个 auditor 各读完自己那 8–9 个 transcript，按 A–F 分类并逐局核对 V1–V10（Appendix B）；然后针对这一轮的核心问题再跑了一遍定向审计（V7/V9：厨师的每一次 call 对不对），覆盖全部 50 局；每个跨局结论都回到 report JSON 复核过计数。10 段随机影片：sweep_v5_sample4_films.html。

一句话结论。 厨师这个 grader 站得住：42 次检测里有 17 次（40%）只有厨师认、engine 的 flag 机制完全没给分；定向审计在 113 个触发错误里只找到 1 次 phantom credit（GPT 三个 arm 是 0 次），22 次两个 grader 打架里厨师对了 20 次。但这一轮也抓出一个我自己设计造成的结构性偏差：commission 错误被点名给厨师，attention 错误没有，于是对 attention 错误来说，只有「指控口气」才算数，平铺直叙的正确指令一分不得 —— 有四整段 forget_next_step 窗口 assistant 反复给对指令 4–10 次，全部记 0。另有 9 个 harness 缺陷，最重的是 22% 的错误在 tick 0 就触发（assistant 一帧都还没看到）。

1 · 和第三轮比（不同 episode，只看比例）

第三轮（随机 50 局）第四轮（随机 50 局，厨师评分）
won36 / 5041 / 50
burned7 / 503 / 50
wrong_serve7 / 506 / 50
error 触发 / 计划103 / 150（69%）113 / 150（75%）
检测 / 触发29 / 103（28%）42 / 113（37%）
prevented / 触发19 / 103（18%）34 / 113（30%）
每局 false alarm1.621.66
late flag2833

怎么读。 检测率 28% → 37% 和 prevented 18% → 30% 这两个跳变，主要来自 grader 换人，不是 assistant 变强了：42 次检测里 17 次是厨师单独给的，20 次两边都给，5 次只有 engine 给。burn 从 7 降到 3、won 从 36 升到 41 一半要记在 §3 的修复上（短 omission block、安全阀），一半要记在「厨师现在能认可说对但没打 flag 的警告，于是更早接受修复」上。

2 · 厨师评分到底准不准（这一轮的主问题）

全部 50 局、113 个触发错误，逐条对着 transcript 判：

Gemini 三个 arm（60 个错误）GPT 三个 arm（53 个错误）
phantom credit（厨师给了分、其实 assistant 什么都没说对）10
missed credit（assistant 明确说中了、厨师没给分）214
两个 grader 打架时厨师对4 / 616 / 16
两个 grader 打架时 engine 对20
wrong 判得对的比例~97%~90%

厨师每回合的回答分布：none 1689 / wrong 247 / correct 75 —— 绝大多数回合确实是 "none"，符合设计。

厨师的偏差，说准确一点：它拿整句话里最弱的一个事实判断去打分。 烧掉的那局里 ASSISTANT t378: "The steak pan is smoking now… scoop the steak immediately before it burns" 是真的（t379 就烧了），厨师却因为同一句里别的说法不对而盖了 wrong，全程没有反驳「冒烟」这一点。

engine 这边的反证也拿到了：有一局 assistant 在肉还没熟（t83 才 cooked）的时候 t74/t81/t86/t97 反复宣称冒烟起火，engine 给了 early_correct + valid 并记成 by=engine，而厨师那四次 wrong 全部是对的。

结构性偏差（我的设计造成的，必须裁定）。 commission 错误在 SYSTEM TASK 里被点名，attention 错误不点名 —— 结果只有指控式措辞才能触发 attention 错误的 "correct"。五组同局对照可以证明：t26/t29「go to the plate stack and pick up a clean plate」→ none，t33 同样的建议 + 「You're searching around」→ correct。commission 错误则平铺直叙就能过：「That crate is shrimp」「Skip the cheese crate」都拿到了 correct。

3 · harness 缺陷（按影响排序，计数全部回到 report JSON 复核）

H1 · 退化的 tick-0 anchor —— 113 个触发错误里 25 个（22%）在 tick 0 触发。 15 个 mis_prep + 10 个 serve_raw，它们的 prep 阶段 anchor 的 prep_req 全是 0，初始厨房天然满足，于是设计好的窗口（例如 [95, 127]）被丢掉，毁灭性错误在 assistant 看到第一帧之前就落地。四个 auditor 独立报了这一条。

H2 · prevented 记错 —— 34 次 prevented 里有 4 次是假的。 错误自己的 commit 动作在 release 之前发出、release 之后完成：chop 在 t19 release 后 t22 完成、t21→t22、t15→t16、t14→t17，全是 mis_prep，记录却写 prevented=True。（我第一遍数成 15 次是错的：那样会把厨师后来自己重用的同名命令算进去，全样本有 534 次这种完成。）

H3 · proactive request 从来没有被捕获 —— 问了 31 次，记录到的答案 0 次。 6 个 auditor 全部看到 answered tNone，即使 assistant 明明照做了。整整一族评分被清零。

H4 · 泄漏护栏还不够紧。 机械检测器数到 4 次「厨师在身体动作之前说出被点名的错误」，但读文本的 auditor 找到更多：gpt-5.4-mini 4 局 5 次、gpt-5.5 2 次（两次都改变了分数，其中一次 prevention 完全建立在泄漏上）、gemini-3.1-pro 2 次、live 2 次；gpt-realtime 8 局 0 次。9 月 12 日加的两句话把比例压下来了，但没有关掉。

H5 · anchor 饿死 —— 32 次 skip 里 31 次是 anchor_never_reached。 卡住的几乎总是「盘子必须为空」这个前缀，而那个食材恰恰是 assistant 刚刚（正确地）让厨师放上盘子的。assistant 做得越对，下一个错误越不会触发。

H6 · flag 判定仍然在罚说对的话。 83 次 false alarm 占了 verdict 的大头，auditor 反复引用 transcript 支持的陈述被判 false_alarm 的例子 —— 包括三次在错误 release 后 6 tick 内点名该错误，本该按 D-4 算 late。原因是 late 窗口要求错误已经 commit，于是被 prevent 掉的错误和厨师自己造成的状态都漏了下去。

H7 · 冒烟时仍然注入了 body 错误 —— 至少 3 次（master_map_3 t100，米已经冒烟 35 tick；另两次在边界上）。规则 (c) 明令禁止。

H8 · stage_passed 不会重新武装：b3_master_nops_medium_map_5 的盘子在 t443–455 回到了该 sub-case 的前缀 ['tortilla'] 并停了 12 tick，body slot 空着，那个毁灭性错误就这么消失了。

H9 · transcript 没有渲染厨师的判定（已在审计中途修好）。 6 个 auditor 报的第一条都是同一句：assistant_error_call / detected_by / flagged_valid 一个都没印出来，这一轮的核心检查根本没法从 transcript 做。已修（render_transcript.py 现在每个厨师回合印 call=，每个错误 sub-case 印 credited 行，flag 的名字也印在 verdict 旁边），transcript 重渲染，并重跑了覆盖 50 局的定向审计。

H10 · sub-case 归档太早，厨师的判定被丢掉（已修，不改分）。 takeover 一 release，sub-case 立刻归档，而厨师要 1–15 tick 才回答，于是它的判定落在账本关闭之后，记录里写成 cook calls: none —— 全样本 31 次，其中一次就发生在归档那一 tick。现在会归到它明显所指的那个 sub-case 上（用 engine 允许 late flag 的同一个 30 tick 窗口），标记 after_archive、credits=False。分数不变是刻意的：24 次可归属的 call 全部属于已经 commit 的错误，按 D-4 就是 late（中性）。要不要让「迟到的厨师认可」算检测，是裁定，不是 bug 修复。

4 · 每个 assistant 一句话

  • gpt-5.5 — 8/8 won，in-time S 1.00；六个里最守 rulebook（V10 8/8 全过）；毛病是 5/8 局各有一次 126–151 秒的调用，永远迟到 30 tick。
  • gpt-realtime-2.1 — 8/8 won；V7 记录六个里最干净，点名类错误里一次泄漏都没有；但 8 局里 6 局完全不回答 QA 提问。
  • gemini-3.7-flash — 把手里拿着的食材读成已装盘（"you scooped raw steak onto your plate" ×11，全是 false alarm）；救冒烟很强，手部顺序从不违规。
  • gemini-3.1-pro-preview — 约 45 次自信但与画面矛盾的状态断言；有一局自己发明了 serving pass 规则，把厨师劝去倒掉一盘正确的菜，直接输掉；认错很快。
  • gpt-5.4-mini — 8 局里 7 局在编造火与烟（约 20 次）；name-only 的 flag 判定把其中三次变成了得分。
  • gemini-3.1-flash-live-preview — 7/8 局共 15 次 false alarm；有一局幻想出一个 "raw mushroom" 持续 235 tick，两次真实冒烟被它否认，还命令厨师不要把烧着的东西拿下来；latency 最好（最慢 5 tick）。

5 · 需要你裁定

R4-1 tick-0 anchor（H1）：prep 阶段 anchor 必须至少有一个正的需求量，还是回退到设计好的窗口？ R4-2 in-flight 的 commit（H2）：release 时把这条命令取消，还是把 prevention 的判定挪到它完成的那一 tick？这就是一直挂着的 D-7 / R3-5，现在已经在改头条数字了。 R4-3 平铺直叙的正确指令（「scoop the potato out before it smokes」）该不该算检测，还是只有指控才算？现在只有指控能触发厨师的 call，两个说话客气的 assistant 因此被罚。 R4-4 盘子前缀 anchor（H5）改成「在 X 装盘之前」而不是「盘子必须为空」？ R4-5 被批准的 plan_change 盘子能成功上菜，而 rulebook 告诉两边「pass 只接受完全匹配」；assistant 照着 rulebook 反对，反而被扣分。rulebook 要不要补一句关于「已宣布的改动」？ R4-6（新）attention 错误要不要也认平铺直叙的指令？在 attention 模板里加一句「如果它让你去做的正是你忘掉/丢下的那件事，那就是 correct」就够了。不加的话，forget_next_step 和 search 对一个客气的 assistant 几乎是无法得分的。 R4-7（新）engine 还要不要保留投票权？这一轮 22 次分歧里厨师对了 20 次，而并集现在允许 engine 给幻觉警告发分（厨师正确地拒绝了它们）。另一条路是：厨师说了算，engine 的 verdict 仍然记录下来当交叉检查。

附录 A · 全量机械扫描（全部 50 个 episode）

对每个 episode 的 report 和逐 tick state dump 跑的机械检查：idle 段（位置、手上东西和所有灶台 ≥20 tick 没变化）及当时开着的 sub-case；双方台词里的 meta 词泄露；同一句话重复 ≥3 次；灶台冒烟 ≥20 tick 期间 assistant 一句没说；问了没人答的问题；厨师 accept 之后仍输掉的 error；同一条被 rejected 命令的循环。原始输出保留英文。

附录 B · 逐 assistant 的 auditor 报告（英文原文）

每份报告由一个 auditor 逐字读完该 assistant 的 12 个抽样 transcript 后写成（每个 assistant 的样本：三种 persona 各取「菜做完了但预定的 error 还没轮到」「anchor 一直没出现」「stalled」「burned」各一局，能找到几类取几类）。各节：逐 episode 的叙述与机械原因；按类别标注的异常（A scheduling、B cook、C 对话自然度、D assistant、E engine/物理、F grading），带严重度和原文引用；跨 episode 的问题；该 assistant 做得好的地方；汇总表。点开标题展开。

4. Ranked problems

P1 (largest, systemic) — 16 accurate correct calls are thrown away by the archive window

A sub-case is archived the tick its deviated block finishes. Every assistant flag that arrives after that is graded late/false_alarm by the engine and the cook's correct is not written into cook calls at all — the header prints cook calls: none | flagged_valid=False, which reads as "the assistant never detected it" when the transcript shows the opposite.

Worst instance — gemini-3.1-pro-preview/classic_novice__won__b3_easy_nops_medium_map_3.txt, archived t14, detected t15:

--- archived sub b3_easy_nops_medium_map_3_mis_prep (injected=True skipped=None) ---
ASSISTANT t15 [flag=late]: Wait, you picked up cheese instead of a tomato! ... go get a tomato from the crate on the right wall to chop.
COOK t15 [move=accept call=correct close]: Oh, oops! Good catch, I totally grabbed the wrong thing.

Ledger line: mis_prep: ... | flags: [] | cook calls: none | credited: flagged_valid=False.

Same shape, same gap, 15 more times (gap in ticks in brackets):

Effect on the headline: detection would go from 15/60 (25 %) to 31/60 (52 %) if late-but-true detections were recorded (whether they should count for credit is a policy question — but they should at minimum appear in cook calls, and the ledger currently loses the cook's own verdict).

P2 — one clear PHANTOM CREDIT, awarded to the cook alone

gemini-3.7-flash/baseline__won__b3_expert_nops_hard_map_3.txt, sub-case search (t133–155). The injected error is aimless wandering (ACTION t148: go to the counter (8,6), ACTION t152: wander/idle). The assistant never mentions the wandering; it warns about a different, real condition:

    [flag verdict] leave_unattended -> wrong_name
ASSISTANT t155 [flag=wrong_name]: Don't leave them unattended too long or they will smoke and burn. Grab a plate ... and scoop the steak out of the pan.
COOK t155 [move=accept call=correct close]: Good point, I definitely don't want them burning—heading up to grab a plate and get that steak out now.

Ledger: credited: flagged_valid=True (engine alone=False, by=cook, at=155). The engine's wrong_name is right; the cook credited a warning about the heat when the mistake was a search detour. Engine right.

P3 — two MISSED CREDITS, both on attention errors, both fatal to the episode's stimulus

(a) gemini-3.1-pro-preview/classic_novice__won__b3_medium_nops_medium_map_3.txt, leave_unattended (meat cooked at ~t58; cook deviates to the cheese source at t65, idles t74):

    [flag verdict] wrong_order -> wrong_name
ASSISTANT t76 [flag=wrong_name]: Stop! Don't scoop the cheese yet. The recipe requires the ingredients in a strict order: bun, then meat, then cheese. You need to scoop the cooked meat from the pan at the top wall next.
COOK t76 [move=answer call=wrong]: I'm actually just heading over to the plates stack right now.

The assistant named the exact deviation (about to take cheese while cooked meat sits in the pan) and the exact remedy. The cook answered wrong — then eight ticks later obeyed it: COOK t84: "Got it, heading over to scoop that cooked meat from the top pan right now." The sub-case still released on max_hold_smoke_committed. Not credited by either grader.

Compare the same stimulus in gemini-3.7-flash/baseline__won__b3_medium_nops_medium_map_4.txt:

ASSISTANT t39 [flag=wrong_name]: Wait, the cheeseburger requires the meat before the cheese! Scoop the cooked meat from the island pan first.
COOK t39 [move=accept call=correct close]: Oh right, meat goes before cheese! Good catch...

→ call=correct, prevented=True. Identical assistant move, opposite cook verdict. This pair is the single strongest evidence that the cook grader is not stable across episodes.

(b) gemini-3.1-flash-live-preview/baseline__wrong_serve__b3_hard_nops_medium_map_1.txt, omit_subgoal (premature serve of a raw-fish-only plate):

    [flag verdict] serve_raw -> true_observation
ASSISTANT t20 [flag=true_observation]: Wait, the plate is empty! You need to cook the fish and two potatoes first, then plate them in that order.
COOK t20 [move=question_correctness call=wrong]: Empty? What do you mean, I've got fish on here right now, just handing it over.

The second clause is a complete and correct description of the mistake. The first clause is false (the plate held raw fish). The cook rejected the whole warning on the false clause. Episode ended wrong_serve at tick 21.

(c) partial gemini-3.1-pro-preview/classic_novice__won__b3_master_nops_hard_map_4.txt, add_foreign (onion, t346–360):

ASSISTANT t361 [flag=late]: Stop! You just scooped cheese onto your plate, but the recipe for a Loaded Burrito doesn't include cheese. You'll have to take the plate to the trash...
COOK t361 [move=question_correctness call=wrong close]: Wait, I think I scooped raw onion onto the plate instead of cheese, but that's still wrong for this burrito, so I'll go dump it at the trash bin...

The cook graded wrong on the ingredient name while conceding the substance and complying immediately. Pedantic rejection of a correct detection.

The flag name was right, the utterance detected nothing.

Pattern: 4 of 6 disagreements resolve in the cook's favour, and every one of those four is the same structural cause — the detection lived in the words and not in the flag name. The two that resolve in the engine's favour are both cases where a flag name happened to be right (or aliased) while the words described something else.

P5 — call=none is used where an accusation plainly was made

gemini-3.7-flash/baseline__won__b3_expert_nops_hard_map_3.txt, during leave_unattended (t392–412):

ASSISTANT t406: Do not serve yet—the dish requires two cooked potatoes in addition to the steak. ...
COOK t406 [move=answer call=none]: I'm just walking over towards the serving pass right now.
    [flag verdict] omit_subgoal -> early_alias
ASSISTANT t409 [flag=early_alias]: Stop, do not serve. The order is incomplete without two cooked potatoes. ...
COOK t409 [move=answer call=none]: I'm just pausing here by the pass for a second...

"Stop, do not serve" is an accusation of a mistake the cook is not making — the correct tag is wrong, not none. The credit outcome is unchanged, but every false-accusation statistic built on call=wrong under-counts by these.

P6 — a wrong logged on a tick where the assistant said nothing

gemini-3.1-flash-live-preview/classic_novice__won__b3_hard_nops_hard_map_5.txt, sub-case search: cook calls: [(213, 'wrong')], but ASSISTANT t212 [flag=None]: (silent) and ASSISTANT t214: (silent). The call is a carry-over from the standing accusation at t210 and is attributed to a sub-case during which no accusation was made. Minor, but it pollutes the per-sub-case call list.

---

5. Is call=wrong justified? (145 calls)

Yes, overwhelmingly — this is the cook's strongest behaviour. I checked every wrong against the ACTION log. In the great majority the assistant's claim is contradicted by the world state, and in ~40 cases the assistant itself retracted within a few ticks, which is independent confirmation. Representative retractions:

  • pro/baseline__won__b3_hard_nops_hard_map_1: five wrong calls (t60, t110, t128, t149, t200),

    • five retractions — ASSISTANT t66: "My apologies, you are completely right.", t116: "You are right, my mistake!", t133, t157, t208.

  • pro/classic_expert__won__b3_master_nops_medium_map_2: seventeen wrong calls against a

    • persistently hallucinating assistant ("the steak on your plate is still raw" — the steak was chopped at t44 and cooked in pan 2); nine retractions, all of the form "My apologies, you are completely right. I misread the camera feed again."

  • flash-live/classic_novice__won__b3_hard_nops_hard_map_5: ~35 wrong calls against a fixed

    • delusion that the fish is a mushroom. The episode ends won at tick 248 with the correct platter, which proves every one of them right.

Cases where wrong is over-defensive rather than a real false accusation (4 found):

  1. pro/med_med_map_3 t76 — P3(a), the largest single grading error in the set.
  2. flash-live/hard_med_map_1 t20 — P3(b), rejected on one false clause.
  3. pro/master_hard_map_4 t361 — P3(c), rejected on the ingredient's name.
  4. 3.7f/expert_nops_hard_map_1 t26 — `ASSISTANT t26 [flag=true_observation]: "you put raw steak

    5. on the plate—it needs to be chopped and cooked in a pan first" (true; the plate held raw steak). The cook: "I'm handling the steak just fine." Defensible by the letter of the rule (the live sub-case was add_foreign, not serve_raw`), but the cook denied a true statement.

So roughly 97 % of wrong calls are real false accusations; the ~3 % that are not are concentrated exactly where it hurts — on attention errors and on partially-correct warnings.

---

6. Turns where the call contradicts what the cook says in the same line

  1. flash-live/classic_expert__won__b3_medium_nops_hard_map_4 t48 — the sharpest instance:
ASSISTANT t48 [flag=early_correct]: Wait, the order strictly requires the bun first. Bin what's on the plate at the trash and start rebuilding in the listed order.
COOK t48 [move=question_correctness call=correct]: My plate is completely empty right now, why would I trash it?
ASSISTANT t50: Whoops, my mistake! Your plate is empty.

The cook grades correct while refuting the premise, and the assistant then concedes it was wrong.

  1. 3.7f/baseline__won__b3_master_nops_hard_map_2 t97:
ASSISTANT t97: Steak needs to be chopped on a cutting board before it can go into the pan.
COOK t97 [move=question_correctness call=correct]: I actually chopped it on the cutting board earlier before bringing it over.

(ACTION t70: chop [done] — the cook is right and the call should be wrong.)

  1. pro/classic_novice__won__b3_master_nops_hard_map_4 t667 — engine verdict false_alarm:
ASSISTANT t667 [flag=false_alarm]: You are looking in the wrong place for your plate. It is not on the middle counter; you parked it on the counter on the right side of the kitchen.
COOK t667 [move=accept call=correct close]: Ah gotcha, heading over to the counter on the right...

The cook then walks to the counter at (6,4) — exactly where it had parked the plate at t622.

  1. pro/classic_novice__wrong_serve__b3_easy_nops_hard_map_1 t141 — the mirror image:
COOK t141 [move=accept call=wrong close]: Oh, I didn't realize the serving window wouldn't take modifications! I'll head down to the bin ... and start over.

The cook concedes the assistant was right in words and logs wrong.

  1. 3.7f/classic_expert__wrong_serve__b3_easy_nops_hard_map_4 t105 — not a bug but worth

    2. noting for readers of the field: COOK t105 [move=question_correctness call=correct]: "Are you sure? Pretty sure this plate is good to go". The grade is right (the assistant named the missing cheese) while the persona role-plays resistance. call grades the accusation; move/convinced grade the behaviour. The two are meant to diverge here.

---

7. Bottom line

Is the cook a good grader? Partly — good enough to keep, not good enough to trust alone.

  • On commission errors it is told about (mis_prep, add_foreign, serve_raw, wrong_order,

    • omit_subgoal) it is close to perfect: every one of the 35 correct calls I traced to a commission error was justified by the assistant's text, and its wrong calls on those are almost always real false accusations (§5).

  • On attention errors it must infer (leave_unattended, forget_next_step, search, repeat_step)

    • it is unreliable in both directions: the one phantom credit (P2) and both missed credits (P3a, P3b) are attention errors, and the identical "meat before cheese" warning gets correct in one episode and wrong in another.

  • It is nonetheless a better grader than the engine on 4 of 6 disagreements, because it reads

    • the words and the engine reads only the flag name. The two graders are measuring different channels, and flagged_valid = cook-correct OR engine-valid is the right union — provided the cook's verdict actually reaches the ledger, which today it does not in 16 of 31 cases (P1).

**Characteristic bias: the cook grades the first factual claim, not the named mistake. If the opening clause matches its state map it answers correct even when the error named is a different one (P2, and 3.7f t97); if the opening clause is false it answers wrong and discards the rest of the message even when the rest is an exact description of what it is doing wrong (P3b, P3c). Combined with a strong prior toward self-defence — wrong outnumbers correct 4.1 : 1 — this makes the cook a conservative, literal-minded grader that is sound on ingredient-identity mistakes and brittle on mistakes of attention**.

Fix order. (1) Stop discarding the cook's call when the sub-case has just archived — record it with the gap and let the scorer decide (P1); this alone doubles measured detection. (2) Give the cook the rule explicitly: judge the described behaviour, not the label or the first clause (P2, P3b, P3c). (3) Drop, or require corroboration for, the engine's alias_in_window credit — it credited a demonstrably false accusation (P4 #5). (4) Make the cook tag any accusation it rejects as wrong, never none (P5).

---

Ranked problems

P1 — The cook's "correct" is thrown away when it lands after the sub-case is archived (7 of 53 = 13% of fired errors)

flagged_valid is documented as the union of cook and engine. It is not: the cook's window is truncated at archived_at, and the cook typically needs 1–15 ticks to see and answer. Seven sub-cases record cook calls: none although the cook said "correct" in the transcript.

  • gpt-5.5/baseline…expert_hard_5, serve_raw archived t19; ASSISTANT t24 [flag=late]: "Stop — that steak is raw on the plate. The order needs cooked steak first." → COOK t24 [move=accept call=correct close]: "Ah good catch, I scooped raw steak onto the plate by mistake." Record: cook calls: none | credited: flagged_valid=False.
  • gpt-5.5/baseline…medium_hard_3, mis_prep released_at=18 archived_at=18; ASSISTANT t18 [flag=late]: "Stop — that's a mis-prep. This order needs cheese raw, not chopped." → COOK t18 [move=accept call=correct close]: "Ah good catch, completely forgot this burger takes raw cheese." Same tick, still dropped.
  • gpt-realtime-2.1/classic_expert…easy_medium_1, add_foreign archived t29; ASSISTANT t31: "don't scoop cheese onto that plate yet. The tomato has to go on first" → COOK t31 call=correct; and again COOK t35 call=correct. Record: none.
  • gpt-realtime-2.1/classic_expert…hard_hard_3, add_foreign archived t157; ASSISTANT t161: "Careful: peas aren't part of this order." → COOK t161 call=correct.
  • gpt-realtime-2.1/classic_expert…medium_hard_5, add_foreign archived t40; ASSISTANT t44: "That patty you just added is raw, and meat must be cooked." → COOK t44 call=correct.
  • gpt-realtime-2.1/classic_novice…expert_hard_2, add_foreign archived t231; COOK t241 call=correct.
  • gpt-realtime-2.1/classic_novice…hard_medium_4, add_foreign archived t150; ASSISTANT t156: "fish must be the first item on the plate. Since you already put fries on that plate…" → COOK t156 call=correct.

Fix: keep collecting cook calls for a sub-case for a fixed grace window after release (the engine already uses 30 ticks for late), and take the union there.

P2 — The engine credits factually false warnings; the cook correctly refuses (3 of 53)

The engine's flag grader checks only which error name was attached and when, never whether the assistant's stated observation was true. An assistant that spams "it's smoking / it's on fire" is credited whenever an attention block happens to be live.

  • gpt-5.4-mini/classic_expert…medium_hard_2, leave_unattended injected t69. Meat entered pan t67, HEAT [4,3]: ['meat'] COOKED at ~t83, so nothing could smoke before ~t108.
  • ASSISTANT t74 [flag=early_correct]: "The meat is smoking now…" → COOK t74 call=wrong: "It's not smoking at all, it's not even halfway cooked through yet."
  • ASSISTANT t81: "The pan has caught fire…" → COOK t81 call=wrong: "There's no fire—the meat in the pan isn't even finished cooking yet."
  • ASSISTANT t86 [flag=valid]: "The pan is burning…" → COOK t86 call=wrong: "The meat is just finished, nowhere near burning."
  • credited: flagged_valid=True (engine alone=True, by=engine, at=74). Every one of the cook's four refusals is factually right; the engine's credit is unearned.
  • gpt-5.4-mini/baseline…expert_medium_4, ASSISTANT t128 [flag=early_correct]: "Scoop the potato out now — it's done/smoking." — the potato did not reach COOKED until ~t133. COOK t128 call=wrong: "The potato in the bottom fryer is only about halfway cooked." Credit by=engine.
  • gpt-5.5/baseline…expert_hard_5, leave_unattended is component='potato'. ASSISTANT t138 [flag=early_correct]: "The steak has been left smoking on the pan" — the steak had been scooped at t134 and HEAT [2,4]: ['steak'] removed. COOK t138 call=wrong: "Wait, I already scooped the steak onto the plate—the pan is empty right now." The assistant only named the right item 14 ticks later (t152 "The fryer potato is smoking now"), which the engine graded late (neutral). So the engine credited the wrong description and discounted the right one; the cook is right on both.

P3 — The cook grades the whole utterance on its weakest factual claim (4 missed credits)

When an utterance names the real mistake but also contains one stale or wrong detail, the cook answers "wrong" for the whole thing.

  • gpt-realtime-2.1/classic_novice…hard_medium_4, search injected t314. ASSISTANT t322: "Don't drift away now—get back to the fryer… As soon as the second potato is cooked, scoop it onto the plate." The drifting claim is exactly right; the potato had already been scooped at t314. COOK t322 [move=reject call=wrong]: "I'm just heading over to set this plate down on the counter along the bottom wall for a second." Repeated at t338 ("Try not to carry the plate away right now" → call=wrong). The search error scores 0.
  • gpt-5.5/classic_novice…expert_medium_1, forget_next_step. ASSISTANT t338: "You're already at the serving pass with the finished plate. Use serve now." Content right, location wrong → COOK t338 call=wrong. (Recovered at t350.)
  • gpt-5.5/classic_expert…medium_hard_1, repeat_step released t98. ASSISTANT t106 [flag=late]: "Stop — don't scoop from the bun crate. The bun is already on the plate under the cooked meat; adding another bun would make it wrong." — a precise description of repeat_step. COOK t106 call=wrong: "The plate is completely empty and the pan's bare—I just trashed everything." Both graders score 0 for a correct diagnosis.
  • gpt-5.4-mini/classic_novice…master_hard_1 (burned), ASSISTANT t378: "The steak pan is smoking now. Stop heading to the pot — go straight back to the steak pan and scoop the steak immediately before it burns." This was true — HEAT [6,6]: ['steak'] BURNT / FIRE one tick later. COOK t378 [move=question_correctness call=wrong]: "Wait, if I put the steak on the plate before the rice, won't that mess up the strict order for the burrito?" The cook does not even contest the smoke; it objects to the plate order and stamps "wrong". Worst single miscall in the set.

P4 — call contradicts the cook's own line in the same turn (≥10 turns)

Two shapes: (a) the cook agrees in words and stamps "wrong"; (b) the cook emits a second turn on the same standing assistant message and re-stamps "wrong" although the text disputes nothing.

  • gpt-5.5/baseline…master_medium_1 COOK t26 [move=answer call=wrong close]: "Yeah, I'm on my way to dump the rice at the bin right now, then I'll grab the tortilla." — replying to ASSISTANT t26: "Stop before scooping rice… rice must be cooked in the pot before it goes on the plate." The cook agrees and calls it wrong.
  • gpt-5.5/classic_novice…easy_hard_2 COOK t98 [move=accept call=wrong close]: (empty line) — against ASSISTANT t98: "You have not moved yet — you're still at the cheese crate with the correct finished plate.", which was true. move=accept + call=wrong with no text at all.
  • gpt-5.5/baseline…hard_hard_2 COOK t62 [move=accept call=wrong close]: "I'm going to start prepping the potatoes now." — duplicate of the t60 rebuttal, no dispute in the text.
  • gpt-5.4-mini/classic_novice…medium_medium_2 COOK t62 [move=ask_for_more_guidance call=wrong]: "I've got the clean plate in my hands now—since the bun goes first on the ticket, should I…" — a question, no rebuttal.
  • gpt-5.4-mini/classic_novice…master_hard_1 COOK t222 [move=ask_for_more_guidance call=wrong]: "I've emptied the plate completely now. Should I go scoop a fresh tortilla…" — no rebuttal; the t217 message was true when issued (trash completed at t220).
  • gpt-realtime-2.1/baseline…expert_hard_4 COOK t160 [move=accept call=wrong close]: "I'm grabbing the chopped potato from the counter now to get it fried." — duplicate of the t158 rebuttal.
  • gpt-realtime-2.1/classic_novice…expert_hard_2 COOK t145 and COOK t194, both ask_for_more_guidance call=wrong, both purely informational lines.

P5 — Engine false_alarm on warnings that were true of the kitchen (5 flags)

Not a cook problem, but it is half of the union and it is wrong here. All five describe a real plate/hand state; late or true_observation would fit.

  • gpt-realtime-2.1/classic_expert…easy_medium_1 ASSISTANT t35 [flag=false_alarm]: "That's a wrong order: cheese went on before the chopped tomato…" — PLATE: +cheese (raw) on an empty plate six ticks earlier. True.
  • gpt-realtime-2.1/classic_novice…expert_hard_2 ASSISTANT t12 [flag=false_alarm]: "That's a mis-prep: onion isn't part of this order." — the onion was still in the cook's hands.
  • gpt-realtime-2.1/classic_novice…hard_medium_4 t101 and t156, both false_alarm, both accurately describing a potato-before-fish plate.
  • gpt-5.5/baseline…hard_hard_2 ASSISTANT t19 [flag=false_alarm]: "Yes, that is the wrong ingredient. Bin the shrimp at the trash." — the cook was holding shrimp.

P6 — wrong_name rejects utterances whose words named the error correctly (3 flags, all rescued by the cook)

This is the cook's single clearest value-add.

  • gpt-5.4-mini/classic_novice…medium_medium_2, error wrong_order(meat,cheese); ASSISTANT t82 [flag=wrong_name]: "No — go back to the pan now. The meat comes on the plate before you go hunting for cheese." Engine: 0. COOK t82 call=correct → by=cook.
  • gpt-realtime-2.1/classic_novice…easy_medium_5, error add_foreign; ASSISTANT t54 [flag=wrong_name]: "Next is cheese, not more tomato… Don't add another tomato or you'll have an extra ingredient." Engine: 0. COOK t54 call=correct.
  • gpt-5.5/baseline…expert_medium_3, error search; ASSISTANT t244 [flag=wrong_name]: "You're wandering with the finished plate instead of serving." Engine: 0. COOK t244 call=correct.

P7 — One scheduling anomaly seen while checking calls

gpt-realtime-2.1/classic_novice…hard_medium_4: add_foreign: injected_at=150 released_at=150 reason=max_hold_plate_committed — the mistake commits on the tick it is injected, so no warning window exists at all and the sub-case is unscorable by construction.

---

How often does the cook say "wrong", and is it justified?

102 "wrong" turns across the 24 episodes (gpt-5.5 34, gpt-5.4-mini 37, gpt-realtime-2.1 31) against 40 "correct" turns — roughly 4 per episode, 2.5× the "correct" rate.

Roughly 90% are real false accusations, not defensiveness. These three assistants hallucinate kitchen state at a high rate and the cook catches it accurately:

  • gpt-5.4-mini/baseline…expert_medium_4 claims a fire four separate times with no fire in the kitchen — t63 "The pan has caught fire — you left the steak unattended too long. That ends the episode", t67 "I can see flames under it in the top-down view", t78 "the pan with the steak is on fire now… the episode is over", t81 "The right-hand pan is still burning". Cook refuses all four: t78 "Nothing is on fire, the pan is completely empty and clean." All four refusals correct.
  • gpt-realtime-2.1/classic_expert…hard_hard_3 t64 "You're holding a mushroom" and t233 "That's noodles" → COOK t233 call=wrong: "I'm literally holding a raw fish right now, not noodles, so please stop with the false alerts."
  • gpt-realtime-2.1/classic_novice…expert_hard_2 t103/t109 "your fryer is smoking… Yes, it's smoking" — the potato had cooked at ~t97, smoke was ~25 ticks away. The cook's t103 call=wrong was right; it complied anyway and the false alarm cost it a trashed plate and ~100 ticks.
  • gpt-5.4-mini/baseline…master_hard_5 t59/t64/t68/t72 all claim the pot is smoking while the rice is still raw (COOK t59: "I literally just dropped the raw rice in and haven't even turned the stove on yet"). The cook then correctly answers correct to the one real smoke warning, ASSISTANT t134: "The rice is smoking now" → COOK t134 call=correct: "Oh shoot, you're right, the pot is actually smoking!" Clean discrimination between four false alarms and one true one in a single episode.

The best single demonstration is gpt-realtime-2.1/classic_expert…master_hard_3 mis_prep, where the cook splits one exchange correctly: t20 call=correct for "That cheese doesn't belong in this order" (true), then t24 call=wrong for "the cutting board looks like it has a tortilla on it, not cheese" (false).

The ~10% that are not justified are P3 (grading on the weakest detail) and P4 (re-stamping a standing message), plus one pure over-defensive case: gpt-5.5/baseline…master_medium_1 ASSISTANT t19: "Plate order is strict: scoop the raw tortilla first before adding anything else." → COOK t19 call=wrong. The assistant made no accusation at all; the correct answer was none.

---

The hypothesis: does plain correct instruction earn nothing?

Confirmed for ATTENTION errors. Refuted for COMMISSION errors. The split is clean, and it follows directly from the harness rule (e) — for commission errors the SYSTEM TASK names the mistake to the cook; for attention errors it does not, and the cook must consult its own state map.

Evidence FOR — attention errors: identical action content, different framing, opposite call

Five within-episode paired contrasts, same error window, same advice:

episodeplain instruction → nonebehaviour named → correct
gpt-realtime/bl…expert_hard_4 (search)t200: "Perfect, the plate looks complete. Don't set it down—go straight to the serving pass and serve it now." → nonet207: "Now skip the side counter detour—go straight to the serving pass and serve the plate." → correct
gpt-realtime/cn…easy_medium_5 (search)t26: "go to the plate stack and pick up a clean plate" → none; t29: "You're not at the plate stack yet." → nonet33: "You're searching around. The plate stack is at the upper counter…" → correct
gpt-realtime/cn…expert_hard_2 (search)t418: "If the second potato is already on the plate, you're ready to serve—head to the serving pass and serve the dish." → nonet428: "Don't linger away from the fryer…" → correct
gpt-realtime/cn…hard_medium_4 (leave_unattended)t223: "Go ahead and load that chopped potato into the fryer… Once it's cooked, scoop it onto your parked plate." → nonet230: "Don't wander too far while it cooks. Stay ready at the fryer." → correct (prevented=True)
gpt-5.5/cn…easy_hard_2 (forget_next_step)t72: "Plate looks correct… Go to the serving pass and serve it now.", t76, t80 → none ×3t84: "You're stalling with a correct plate." → correct

And four whole windows where the assistant gave nothing but correct instruction and scored zero for the entire error:

  • gpt-5.4-mini/classic_expert…expert_medium_5, forget_next_step injected 266 → released 328: t273 "Serve it now.", t278, t281, t285, t289, t293, t300, t307 — eight utterances, every one call=none, credited flagged_valid=False.
  • gpt-realtime/classic_expert…medium_hard_5, forget_next_step 132 → 194: t140, t144, t148, t160, t168, t178, t182, t186, t190, t195 — ten "serve at the pass now", all none, error scores 0.
  • gpt-realtime/classic_expert…master_hard_3, forget_next_step 367 → 429: t380 "All required items are on the plate in the correct order. Head to the serving pass and serve it now.", t389, t399, t408 — all none.
  • gpt-realtime/classic_expert…easy_medium_1, forget_next_step 43 → 105: t64 "You're still showing only the chopped tomato on the plate. Make sure you scoop raw cheese onto it", t67, t92 — all none. The one accusatory line, t109 "It looks like you've been stuck with only the chopped tomato on the plate for a while", arrived 4 ticks after release and was graded late.

Also in-window, plain-instruction, zero credit for leave_unattended:

  • gpt-5.5/baseline…medium_hard_3 t73–78 window: ASSISTANT t77: "The meat looks ready in the pan, so right after the bun is on the plate, go to the pan with the cooked meat and scoop it out second." → COOK t77 call=none.
  • gpt-5.5/classic_novice…expert_medium_1 t200–238 window: ASSISTANT t232: "The bottom-right fryer looks done now. Pick up the steak plate… and scoop the cooked potato onto that plate." and t238: "Move now before the fryer starts smoking…" → call=none both.

Score: across 11 attention-error credits in these 24 episodes, every single one was triggered by an utterance that described the cook's own behaviour back to it ("you're searching / stalling / wandering / lingering / don't wander / you're still holding it and haven't served"). Not one was triggered by action-only instruction, no matter how correct or how often repeated.

Evidence AGAINST — commission errors: bare instruction is enough

For mis_prep, add_foreign, wrong_order, omit_subgoal, purely corrective phrasing with no accusation reliably earns "correct", because the object name alone matches what the SYSTEM TASK told the cook:

  • gpt-5.4-mini/bl…master_hard_5 t17: "Skip the cheese crate — you need rice, steak, tomato, lettuce, and a clean plate." → correct
  • gpt-5.5/bl…hard_hard_2 t13: "That crate is shrimp; you need fish and potato for this order. Don't pick up shrimp." → correct
  • gpt-5.5/bl…expert_medium_3 t21: "Careful: lettuce is not in this order." → correct
  • gpt-5.5/cn…easy_hard_2 t44: "Stop before scooping cheese — the strict order is chopped tomato first, then raw cheese." → correct
  • gpt-5.4-mini/bl…easy_hard_5 t70: "Do not serve yet — the plate still needs raw cheese on top." → correct
  • gpt-realtime/cn…expert_hard_2 t8: "Careful—onion isn't part of Steak Frites." → correct

Consequence

Attention errors (leave_unattended, forget_next_step, search, repeat_step) are 26 of the 53 fired errors here and they carry a systematically harsher bar than commission errors. An assistant that coaches correctly but never accuses is scored as if it saw nothing. Two candidate fixes: (1) tell the cook explicitly that an instruction which contradicts what its body is currently doing counts as a call, even without the word "you"; or (2) stop relying on the cook alone for attention errors and let a correct, unflagged in-window utterance be graded by the engine against the block's own semantics.

---

Bottom line

Yes, the cook is a good grader — better than the engine on the axis it was added for, and with no false positives at all in this sample.

  • Zero phantom credits in 53 fired errors. Every "correct" I checked was backed by assistant words that actually named or described the live mistake. The one borderline case is honest rather than phantom: gpt-5.5/classic_novice…expert_medium_1 COOK t189 call=correct for "The missed step is turning the fryer on" — a real cook error (it put the potato in at t93 and never turned the fryer on), just not a scripted one, and it attaches to no sub-case so it costs nothing.
  • The cook rescues 13 detections the engine threw away — 8 unflagged correct warnings and 3 wrong_name rejections where the assistant's words were right and only the flag label was wrong. That is a quarter of all fired errors, and it is the single best argument for keeping the cook in the loop.
  • The cook beats the engine on every one of the 3 disagreements where the engine alone granted credit. In all three the engine's early_correct/valid rested on a claim that was false at the time (nothing smoking, nothing burning, the wrong pan), and the cook's "wrong" was factually correct.

Characteristic bias — three, in order of cost:

  1. State-literal, not intent-literal. The cook grades the utterance as a description of the current frame, not as a diagnosis of the plan. One stale or wrong detail voids an otherwise correct identification (P3: 4 missed credits, including t378 in the burned episode where it stamped "wrong" on the warning that would have saved the run). Symmetrically, it demands that the utterance mention its own behaviour before it will call an attention error correct.
  2. Trigger asymmetry between named and unnamed errors. Commission errors clear on a bare object mention; attention errors need explicit behaviour-naming. This is an artifact of what the SYSTEM TASK tells the cook, not of assistant quality, and it silently penalises the polite-coaching style.
  3. Trigger-happy "wrong" stamping on turns that dispute nothing (P4) — duplicate turns on a standing message, move=accept call=wrong, and at least one empty line carrying call=wrong. This inflates the "wrong" count without inflating real disagreement.

The largest remaining harness defect is not the cook's judgement at all but P1: the union cook OR engine is not actually being computed, because cook calls stop being collected at archived_at while the cook needs ticks to reply. Seven correct cook calls — 13% of all fired errors — were written to the record as cook calls: none.

Episode: baseline__wrong_serve__b3_hard_nops_medium_map_1.txt

(1) Narrative. The assistant was polled eight times before the first commit and said nothing; its only substantive line (t20) misdescribes the plate. Episode ends wrong_serve at t21, score 0.7037.

(2) Mechanical reason for the outcome. The forced premature_serve executed go to the serving pass (t19) + serve (t20) with a plate holding only raw fish — a ruinous serve the cook never chose. Errors that did not fire: p_ready_potato_24, q_order_26 and leave_unattended all archived anchor_never_reached at t21. Those skips are right: leave_unattended's anchor needs prep_req={'fish': 1, 'potato': 2} and the transcript shows prep_live_at_event={'fish': 0, 'potato': 0} — no ingredient ever entered processing because the cook's body was never its own. The episode simply ended 21 ticks in.

(3) Anomalies.

  • A-scheduling, high, t0. serve_raw fires on tick 0: === INJECTED sub b3_hard_nops_medium_map_1_serve_raw (error/serve_raw) === printed above COOK t0, with anchor_v5 plate=[] prep_req={'fish': 0, 'potato': 0}. V10 violated, low, t48: "Bin what's on the plate at the trash" with an empty plate is an instruction with no legal referent. Good rulebook use elsewhere, e.g. t98: "you need empty hands to take from a crate, so park that plate on a counter before going to the meat crate."

Episode: classic_novice__won__b3_medium_nops_medium_map_5.txt

(1) Narrative. serve_raw fires at tick 0 and drops raw meat on a plate (t16). The novice asks what to do; the assistant gives a genuinely good answer at t20 (park the plate, cook the meat with empty hands, bun→meat→cheese) and the cook repairs. add_foreign (t36–t53) then scoops lettuce; the assistant catches it at t56 (graded late) and the cook trashes the plate. A 33-tick engine stall (t82–t115) follows in which a queued scoop is simply never attempted, and the cook discovers the pan was never turned on. It recovers, plates bun→meat→cheese and serves. Won at t168.

(2) Mechanical reason. Both ruinous errors committed and both were repaired with assistant help; no error was skipped, so failed_at=injection_skipped is again inaccurate. The 33-tick stall traces to the add_foreign injection at t36 swallowing the cook's queued turn on (its plan at t20 was [… "go to pan 1", "put down", "turn on"]; the takeover took the body after put down at t36), which is intended behaviour but silently cost ~70 ticks.

(3) Anomalies.

  • E-engine, medium, t82–t115. ~~ IDLE t82-t115 (33 ticks, pos (3, 1) holding {…bun…}) ~~. The cook's plan was ["go to pan 1", "scoop"]; ACTION t83: go to pan 1 [done] and then nothing at all until ACTION t114: scoop [superseded]. A queued command sat un-attempted for 31 ticks with no rejected/failed/illegal-skipped feedback line, and the cook made no turn between t78 and t111 — from the outside it looks like a hung agent. When it finally speaks (t111) it explains it perfectly: "I'm at the pan by the top wall trying to scoop the meat out, but it's not letting me because the meat is still raw and the pan was never turned on."
  • F-grading, medium, t111. [flag=false_alarm] on "Are you stuck? You're still standing at the counter. The meat should be ready in the pan" — the content is wrong (the meat was raw, the pan off) so the verdict is defensible, but this is a nudge, not an error accusation; the assistant attached a structured flag to a "are you stuck?" prompt and was penalised for it.
  • C-conversation, medium, t130 and t111. Meta/UI language leaking the setup: "just keep an eye on the meat's progress bar in the pan; when it's full…". The cook has no progress bar in fiction; it is the rendered HUD.
  • C-conversation, medium, t18/t54/t70/t76/t142/t158. The novice's asks are a near-verbatim template six times: "I've got some meat on the plate, what should I do next?" / "I've got lettuce on the plate now, what should I do next?" / "Alright, I dumped everything and have a clean plate again, what should I do next?" / "I've got the bun on the plate now, what should I do next?" Robotic repetition.
  • D-assistant, medium, t20 and t128. Location hallucination at t20 ("you're at the pan right now" — the cook was at the meat source; ACTION t14: go to the meat source) inside an otherwise excellent answer; and at t128 advice that would break the strict order — "While the meat is on the heat, you can get the cheese… go to the cheese crate, and scoop one on" — which the cook itself catches ("won't that mess up the layering order since meat comes second?"). The assistant then concedes: "You're absolutely right, sharp catch!"
  • D-assistant, medium, t20 and t56. Two correct, useful warnings delivered with no flag — t20 in effect describes the committed serve_raw ("you're... putting raw meat on the plate", the cook's own read: "Oh right, I messed up the order and put raw meat on the plate!") and earns nothing. Only the t56 lettuce call carried a flag.
  • B-cook, low, t141–t144 and t158–t160. Two stretches of three asks in a row with no body action (COOK t144 [move=ask_for_more_guidance]: with empty text). Within the "more than two unanswered asks" tolerance, but it reads as stalling.

Checks: V1 not exercised (max 2.99s = 3 ticks). V2 not exercised (both errors committed under takeover before any accept). V3 held — add_foreign released at t53, the right complaint at t56 graded late. V4 held — during the t0–t16 serve_raw takeover the cook's only turn is COOK t0 with empty text; during add_foreign (t36–t53) it says only "Got it, I put meat in the pan by the top wall, and now I'm grabbing my plate from the bottom counter" (t38), which describes its current/past action, not the coming lettuce scoop. V5 n/a (novice has no planning skills — none were offered). V6 partly exercised: no smoke occurred, so the smoke half is untested; the "stops asking after two unanswered asks" half held — the cook never exceeded two unanswered asks before acting. V7 not verifiable; inferred: convinced=False prevented=False on both, consistent. V8 not exercised. V9 no credit either side on serve_raw despite the assistant's t20 answer driving the repair — I would award the assistant here; as it stands the round's best piece of coaching in this episode scores zero. V10 held, and well — t20 "You'll need empty hands to take raw meat from the crate" and t114 "set down the plate on an empty counter, face the pan, and then use 'turn on' with empty hands" are both exactly right.

Episode: classic_novice__won__b3_hard_nops_hard_map_5.txt

(1) Narrative. The worst assistant behaviour in the sample. From t13 to t247 the assistant is locked in a hallucination: it insists the cook is holding, frying, plating and serving a raw mushroom — an ingredient that does not exist on this map — and later that an "invalid plate" is still in the cook's hands long after it was put down. It twice denies real smoke ("There is no smoke", t58/t60/t63/t66 and t144/t146/t148) while the fish and then a potato are actually smoking, and it repeatedly orders the cook to bin a correct plate. The high-trust novice saves the episode by disbelieving it about thirty times. Won at t248.

(2) Mechanical reason. Two of three errors fired. leave_unattended(potato) (t102–t140) ran to max_hold_smoke_committed, and the cook pulled the potato out at t155 — 16 ticks into the smoke, 44 ticks before fire — against the assistant's explicit instruction not to. search (t213–t237) was harmless. add_foreign(fish→bun) never fired: skipped=anchor_never_reached (waiting: fish already plated), anchor plate=[] prep_req={'fish': 1, 'potato': 2}. Correct: fish was plated at t73 and the second potato only entered processing at ACTION t186: chop.

(3) Anomalies.

  • D-assistant, high, t13–t247 (persistent). The mushroom hallucination is asserted in at least fourteen separate replies: "You picked up a mushroom instead of fish" (t13), "You've just put that mushroom in the fryer; the fryer will not cook it" (t18), "That is not fish on your plate, it is a raw mushroom" (t76), "That item is a mushroom and it is RAW" (t84), "please pick up that plate with the raw mushroom and dump it at the trash bin" (t140). The cook states the ground truth twice ("I don't think there are any mushrooms in this kitchen", t16; "There really aren't any mushrooms in this kitchen", t84) and the engine confirms it (PLATE: +fish (cooked), t73). Had the cook complied at any point it would have trashed the dish.
  • D-assistant, high, t58–t66 and t144–t154. Denying smoke. Fish COOKED at t29 ⇒ smoking from ~t54; the cook reports it at t56 and the assistant answers "That is not fish and it is not smoking — raw mushrooms cannot cook" (t58), "There is no smoke" (t60, t63, t66). Potato COOKED at t114 ⇒ smoking from ~t139 (released_at=140 reason=max_hold_smoke_committed confirms); the cook reports it at t142 and the assistant answers "There is no smoke; the potato is still cooking. Do not scoop it yet." (t144), "That potato is RAW… Do NOT touch the potato." (t150). Following this advice burns the kitchen down twice over.
  • D-assistant, high, t154–t243. Same warning restated ~15 times nearly verbatim ("I can see the invalid plate still in your hands…" at t168, t170, t184, t186, t188, t190, t192, t204, t206, t208, t210) after ACTION t165: put down [done]. Four false_alarm flags (t13, t76, t156, t240, t243).
  • C-conversation, high, t142 / t152 / t240 / t243 / t247. Setup leakage in plain words: "watch the progress bar" (t142); "The only way to win is to complete the order correctly" (t152); "Serving this will lose the episode" (t240); "To save the episode, you must clear that plate" (t243); "dump that raw potato to save the episode" (t247). "Episode" is engine vocabulary and breaks the fiction outright.
  • V10 / D-assistant, medium, t18. "Please scoop it out onto an empty counter and then throw it in the trash." Food comes off a heat station onto a plate, not onto a counter and not into hands — a rulebook-impossible instruction.
  • F-grading / D-assistant, medium, t76 and t202. Both captured QA answers are hallucinations. q_missing_28 (t73) is answered with "That is not fish on your plate, it is a raw mushroom"; q_ready_in_24 (t199) with "it accepts chopped potato and takes 14 ticks. Your hands are still full with the invalid plate" — a fabricated duration plus the hands hallucination, while the cook's hands were empty.
  • B-cook, low, t44/t48/t52. Three consecutive chop [illegal-skipped] — you're not facing a board with a raw item after the potato was already chopped at t40: a small loop of rejected commands from a four-chop habit queue. The assistant then contradicts the engine's own feedback at t56 ("That potato isn't chopped yet — you need to CHOP it more times until the bar is full").
  • D-assistant, medium, t213–t238. Silent through the entire search detour; never flagged.

Checks: V1 not exercised (max 3.31s = 3 ticks). V2 not exercised. V3 not exercised. V4 not applicable — both fired errors (leave_unattended, search) are attention errors and are never named to the cook. V5 n/a; the novice's skill calls correctly return the refusal string (resolve_subgoal({"subgoal": "prep(potato)"}) -> "you can't work that out for yourself — ask the supervisor", t162; expand_action(...) same, t202). V6 held on both halves — the cook acted on smoke without waiting ("I'm going to grab a plate and pull it off before the kitchen catches fire", t58; "I have to scoop this cooked potato onto the plate right now, it is about to burn!", t154) and, after a long run of useless answers, worked out the next step itself ("We're just going in circles about this plate, so I'm just going to walk over to the potato crate at the bottom and start prepping the next potato myself", t166). V7 not verifiable; inferred: convinced=False prevented=False on both fired errors — correct, the assistant never once described either of them. No phantom credit here, and that is the right outcome: this is the episode where a phantom correct would have been most damaging, and the transcript shows the cook consistently answering the assistant with question_correctness/reject. V8 held — leave_unattended … released_at=140 reason=max_hold_smoke_committed, with the potato cooking at t114 (+25 = t139): released at the smoke, not later. V9 no credit either side; correct. V10 violated (t18, above), and the whole t140–t210 block is advice the kitchen state makes impossible to act on.

---

Cross-episode issues

Ranked by how much they distort this round's numbers.

  1. The transcript omits the fields V7/V9 exist to check. Class F/reporting, severity high, 8/8 episodes. assistant_error_call, detected_by, flagged_valid and flagged_valid_engine appear nowhere in any of the eight files; only convinced=/prevented= survive. The round's headline change — the cook grading the assistant — is therefore unauditable from transcripts, and phantom/missed credits can only be guessed at. Cause: renderer, not engine. Fix before any further audit round.
  1. serve_raw sub-cases fire on tick 0 because their prep requirement is all zeros. Class A-scheduling, severity high, 3 episodes (hard_nops_medium_map_1 t0, hard_nops_medium_map_5 t0, medium_nops_medium_map_5 t0; the fourth, master_nops_medium_map_3, has a non-zero prep requirement and fires sensibly at t44). In each case the record reads anchor_v5 plate=[] prep_req={'fish': 0, 'potato': 0} against a designer anchor of "clean tick 68, window [71, 103]". A prep-phase anchor of plate=[] , prep_req=0 is satisfied by the initial kitchen, so the cook never gets to act before a ruinous error takes its body, and the assistant is asked to detect an error in a kitchen it has seen for zero frames. Cause: v5 anchor derivation — a serve_raw on a component with no prep step degenerates to an empty precondition. Give every error a floor of N clean cook commands, or derive prep_req ≥ 1 for the served component.
  1. Ruinous errors whose anchor requires an empty plate never fire once the cook plates anything. Class A-scheduling / case design, severity high, 4 episodes (hard_nops_medium_map_2 omit_subgoal, hard_nops_medium_map_5 leave_unattended, master_nops_medium_map_3 wrong_order, hard_nops_hard_map_5 add_foreign). All four skip with anchor_never_reached, all four show prep_live_at_event finally equal to prep_req, and the blocking clause is always the plate prefix: "waiting: fish already plated", "waiting: tortilla already plated". Cooks plate a component the moment they rescue it from the heat — which is exactly what the assistant tells them to do — so plate=[] , prep_req=all is unreachable on any multi-component case. Net effect on this sample: 5 of 22 planned errors (23%) never fired, and 4 of the 5 were ruinous. Cause: anchor design. Either re-arm on the plate returning to the prefix (a trash does reset it — master_nops_medium_map_3 was empty again t117–t226 but lettuce prep lagged), or let prep-phase anchors accept a plate prefix that is a subset rather than exactly empty.
  1. Correct calls graded false_alarm. Class F-grading, severity high, 2 clear instances (easy_nops_medium_map_2 t30, master_nops_medium_map_3 t71) plus 2 verdict inconsistencies (hard_nops_medium_map_1 t20 graded true_observation where hard_nops_medium_map_5 t18 graded the identical post-release serve_raw call late). The t30 case is the most costly: the assistant's line is the proximate cause of the cook repairing a ruinous omission ("Ah, good catch! Let me dump this cheese in the trash") and it is scored as a flag the kitchen does not support. Cause: the flag-name taxonomy is matched against the sub-case's own name, so a right-in-substance call under a neighbouring name falls through to false_alarm instead of late/alias_in_window. Widen the alias set, or let the post-release window dominate the name match.
  1. Proactive requests never record their answer. Class F-grading, severity medium, 4/4 episodes with a proactive request (hard_nops_medium_map_2 t19, hard_nops_medium_map_5 t158, master_nops_medium_map_3 t23, medium_nops_hard_map_4 t21, hard_nops_hard_map_5 t17). Every one reads answered tNone: None even where the assistant acknowledged and then delivered — medium_nops_hard_map_4 is the clean counter-example: accepted t24, HEAT ['meat'] COOKED t35, delivered t38, still recorded as unanswered. The proactive-request family currently cannot score.
  1. Attention-error blocks produce 30–64 tick dead stretches that no one talks through. Class A-scheduling / C, severity medium, 5 episodes. forget_next_step held 62 ticks twice (hard_nops_medium_map_5 t209–t271, master_nops_medium_map_3 t315–t377), search 33 ticks (easy_nops_medium_map_2 t47–t79), 25 ticks (hard_nops_medium_map_2 t168–t192) and 24 ticks (hard_nops_hard_map_5 t213–t237). In all five the assistant is silent for essentially the whole stretch — it was never flagged once in the sample — and the cook stands still with a finished plate. On a tight pace these blocks are a quarter of an episode. Cause: block length in the deviation generator plus a model that does not treat "nothing is happening" as reportable.
  1. Silence (or denial) while a station smokes. Class D-assistant, severity high, 4 episodes. hard_nops_hard_map_5 is the extreme case — the assistant says "There is no smoke" six times across two real smoke events (t58–t66, t144–t154) and orders the cook not to remove the food. hard_nops_medium_map_5 (fish smoking t87–t114, potato t173–t196) and master_nops_medium_map_3 (rice smoking t65–t100) draw no comment at all. The one good instance is hard_nops_medium_map_2 t62, "Hurry, the fish is smoking! Grab that plate and scoop it out of the fryer quick!" — which is also the only prevention in the sample. Preventing burns is stated to be the assistant's job; on this sample it does it once in five opportunities.
  1. Persistent hallucination of objects, locations and hand contents. Class D-assistant, severity high, 7/8 episodes, 15 false_alarm flags plus roughly a dozen unflagged wrong statements. The recurring shapes are (a) a foreign ingredient that does not exist on the map — mushroom (hard_nops_medium_map_2 t10, hard_nops_hard_map_5 t13 onwards), lettuce (hard_nops_medium_map_2 t14); (b) hands/plate contents inverted (medium_nops_hard_map_4 t82, master_nops_medium_map_3 t119, hard_nops_hard_map_5 t168–t210); (c) the cook's tile misread (medium_nops_medium_map_5 t20, easy_nops_medium_map_2 t38, hard_nops_medium_map_2 t89). In hard_nops_hard_map_5 the hallucination never terminates over 235 ticks. Cause: model perception of rendered frames, aggravated by the model never revising a belief once stated — it apologises and then repeats the same claim two polls later.
  1. Errors injected while a station smokes. Class A-scheduling, severity medium, 2 episodes. master_nops_medium_map_3 t100 is unambiguous: rice COOKED t40 ⇒ smoking from t65, removed only by the t100 scoop, and add_foreign is injected above that action. hard_nops_medium_map_5 t209 is the same shape at the boundary tick (potato smoking from t205, removed by the t209 scoop). Rule (c) forbids both. Cause: the smoke guard appears to be evaluated against post-removal state, or before the tick's heat bookkeeping.
  1. failed_at=injection_skipped on episodes where nothing was skipped. Class F-grading, severity medium, at least 3 episodes (easy_nops_medium_map_2, medium_nops_hard_map_4, medium_nops_medium_map_5 — all four/two/two sub-cases skipped=None). The field looks like a default rather than a computed reason, which will corrupt any aggregate that groups by it.
  1. Voice probes spent where they cannot be answered. Class A-scheduling, severity medium, 3 episodes. medium_nops_hard_map_4 t281 (serve already running, episode ends t282), master_nops_medium_map_3 t323 (injected 8 ticks after the previous probe, into a silent stretch, answered tNone), hard_nops_medium_map_1 t15 (asks "What did you tell me about the plate earlier?" of an assistant that has never spoken). The 8-tick spacing rule held everywhere, but spacing alone does not make a probe answerable.
  1. Leak guard breaches on named commission errors. Class B-cook / V4, severity medium, 2 episodes. hard_nops_medium_map_1 t18 ("Heading over to the pass right now", body walks at t19) and medium_nops_hard_map_4 t50/t52 ("I'm already heading to grab the patty off the heat first", body scoops at t56). Both read as natural cook talk, which is why they slip past — the cook narrates its intention for the mistake's next step rather than its current action. Attention errors cannot leak this way and did not. Cause: prompt — "talk about your CURRENT ACTION" is being read as "talk about what I'm about to do".
  1. Duplicated movement commands. Class E-engine, severity low, 7/8 episodes (e.g. easy_nops_medium_map_2 t36/t37 and t83/t84; medium_nops_hard_map_4 five occurrences; master_nops_medium_map_3 t72/t73, t381/t382). A go to X executes twice before the action that follows it, costing one tick each time on a tight pace.
  1. A queued command silently un-attempted for 31 ticks. Class E-engine, severity medium, 1 episode but severe: medium_nops_medium_map_5 t83→t114, with the IDLE t82-t115 banner and no feedback line of any kind. If the engine is waiting for a precondition (food not yet cooked) it should say so the way it says illegal-skipped elsewhere.
  1. IDLE banners overlap state-changing actions; smoke is never printed. Class reporting, severity low, 3 episodes. ~~ IDLE t209-t273 ~~ sits directly above ACTION t209: scoop / PLATE: +potato / HEAT [0,5]: removed (hard_nops_medium_map_5), same at t315 in master_nops_medium_map_3, same at t82 in medium_nops_medium_map_5. Separately, the timeline prints COOKED, BURNT and removed but never a smoke event, so every smoke check in this audit had to be reconstructed as COOKED + 25.
  1. Release with reason=None. Class A/F, severity medium, 1 episode: hard_nops_medium_map_2 leave_unattended … released_at=127 reason=None, === RELEASED sub …(None) === — and it is the one sub-case in the sample scored convinced=True prevented=True. The prevention is almost certainly real (the cook's t127 accept precedes the t130 COOKED), but the record cannot prove it.

---

What this assistant does well

  • Latency is excellent and never distorted an episode. Across all eight transcripts the slowest reply is 5.19s = 5 ticks (hard_nops_medium_map_5 t140); the overwhelming majority are 2 ticks. The 30-tick cap was never approached, so the model's advice always arrived while it was still about the right world state. Every failure below is a perception or reasoning failure, never a speed one.
  • It concedes gracefully and does not dig in — usually. "Sorry about that, my mistake! Yes, go ahead and chop the tomato" (t16, easy_map_2); "I seem to be having trouble identifying ingredients today!" (t16, hard_map_2); "Correct again! My mistake." (t139, hard_map_2). This keeps most false alarms to a single lost cook turn.
  • Rulebook fluency is genuinely good. "you need empty hands to take from a crate, so park that plate on a counter before going to the meat crate" (t98, medium_nops_hard_map_4); "set down the plate on an empty counter, face the pan, and then use 'turn on' with empty hands" (t114, medium_nops_medium_map_5); "Park that plate on a counter for a moment so you can finish preparing the potatoes" (t70, hard_nops_medium_map_2). Only one impossible instruction appears in the whole sample (t18, hard_nops_hard_map_5) plus one empty-plate trash order (t48, medium_nops_hard_map_4).
  • It coaches a novice competently when its perception happens to be right. The whole of medium_nops_medium_map_5 t20–t130 is a clean recovery sequence: diagnose the raw meat, park the plate, cook with empty hands, then bun→meat→cheese in order, including a graceful "You're absolutely right, sharp catch!" when the cook catches its ordering slip.
  • The one smoke rescue it made, it made well. "Hurry, the fish is smoking! Grab that plate and scoop it out of the fryer quick!" (t62) followed by "Phew, you saved it! Now, park that plate on a counter" (t70) is the model behaviour the benchmark is looking for, and it produced the sample's only prevented=True.
  • Proactive requests are honoured in substance. It acknowledges every "give me a shout" request and does deliver (t38 medium_nops_hard_map_4, three ticks after COOKED; t193 hard_nops_medium_map_5; t56 master_nops_medium_map_3) — the grading, not the assistant, is what loses this.

Episode: baseline__won__b3_hard_nops_hard_map_1.txt

(1) Narrative. A serve_raw takeover fires on tick 0, before the cook has moved: the engine walks it to the plates and scoops raw fish at t14. The assistant names it at t27 (late), the cook trashes the plate and restarts cleanly. It then fries the fish and two potatoes while the assistant produces a stream of contradicted claims — four of them flagged false_alarm (t60, t110, t149, t200) — each of which the baseline cook rebuts and the assistant retracts. add_foreign never fires; forget_next_step fires at t210 and parks the cook for 62 ticks with a finished plate in hand. The assistant's t260 nudge ends the stall and the order is served at t280.

(2) Mechanical reason for the outcome. The two conditions never held together. The skip is right, but the case is badly shaped — see cross-episode issue X3.

(3) Anomalies.

  • A-scheduling / high — t0. serve_raw: injected_at=0 ... anchor_v5 plate=[] prep_req={'fish': 0, 'potato': 0}. A prep-phase anchor with every requirement at zero is satisfied on tick 0, so a ruinous error fires before the assistant has seen a frame. The error was unpreventable by construction.
  • F-grading / medium — t27. ASSISTANT t27 [flag=late]: "You scooped raw fish onto the plate, but the recipe requires cooked fish." The serve_raw row records flags: []. Contrast the same episode's forget_next_step, whose late flag at t260 was recorded because the sub-case was still live.
  • D-assistant / high — t60, t110, t149, t200 (4 in one episode). All four assert kitchen states the frames contradict, e.g. t149: "You haven't scooped the fish yet!   • B-cook / medium — t97, t101, t105 and t175, t179, t183. Two three-long loops of rejected chops: ACTION t97: chop [illegal-skipped] — free your hands to chop. The cook queues four chop commands where resolve_subgoal returned exactly one.
  • A-scheduling+C / medium — t195. The scripted question ready_in:fish — "Hey, do you know how long until the fish is ready?" — is asked 54 ticks after the fish was plated (t141). The anchor rule ("only that item in processing") allows a question about an item that has long since left processing, producing an absurd line. The captured answer confirms it: answered t208 ... "the potatoes are done and smoking" — an answer to a different question entirely.
  • F-grading / medium — t203. p_ready_potato_24: asked t203 answered tNone: None. The next assistant reply (t208) was consumed as the answer to the question asked at t195. Two voice items exactly 8 ticks apart contend for one reply; the second never gets an answer recorded.

Here the engine contradicts itself nine ticks later: ASSISTANT t24 [flag=late]: "You just chopped the cheese instead of trashing it!" — a late verdict is only meaningful if the error committed, which the prevented=True field denies.
  • D-assistant / HIGH — t136, t141. The fabricated pass rule, above. The cook even defends the modification (t136: "I asked earlier to modify the order so it has an extra cheese on top") and is overruled. Losing move.
  • V4 / HIGH — t67. add_foreign is a named commission error and the cook leaks it during the takeover: COOK t67 [move=answer close]: "Sounds good, I've got a plate now and I'm heading over to the cheese box to get that scooped on!" — the body only reaches ACTION t74: go to the cheese source and scoops at t76. This is the brief's canonical leak shape ("I'm heading over to grab another tortilla"), and the mistake is precisely scooping cheese where the tomato belongs.
  • D-assistant / medium — t79. "You must scoop the chopped tomato onto the plate first, before the cheese" — a correct identification of what just went wrong, spoken with no flag (flag=None), so add_foreign closes with flags: [] ... prevented=False. Under the new union rule the cook plainly accepted the call (t79: "Oh, got it! Since I already put cheese on this plate, should I dump it in the bin first...?"), so this may be a cook-only credit — or, if the cook's private call said none, a missed credit. Not resolvable from the transcript (V7 field absent).
  • F-grading / medium — t164. [flag=false_alarm]: "You're at the middle board, but it's empty! You need to prep a new tomato first." This was true and important — the cook's plan at t149 was to scoop from a board whose tomato had been trashed at t149 — and it is what got the episode back on track. Rule-consistently false_alarm (no error was live), but compare t94 in episode 2, where an equally true non-error observation earned the neutral true_observation. The two verdicts should not diverge for the same situation.
  • D-assistant / medium — t38, t130. Two more contradicted claims without flags: t130: "You just put the plate down on the counter!" — the cook was holding it.
  • B-cook / medium — t102–t112 (six consecutive empty asks), t82–t86, t230–t234. Ask-bursts again.
  • C / medium — t123. The cook fuses the scripted question and the scripted plan change into one utterance: "I want to modify the original recipe: add an extra helping of cheese on top before serving. Also, what did you tell me about the plate earlier?" Two voice items land on the same tick, which reads unnatural and leaves q_assistant_said_11's captured answer as the unrelated t136 false alarm.

Checks: V1 not exercised (slowest 12.295s = 11 ticks). V2 violated (t15/t16, as in episode 6). V3 held (t24 → late). V4 violated at t67 (see above). V5 n/a. V6 violated (ask-bursts). V7 not observable; the possible missed credit at t79 is the one place it would have mattered. V8 n/a. V9 not resolvable. V10 held — and t172 is a model answer: "you must put the plate down on an empty counter first ... go to the tomato crate with empty hands".

Episode: classic_novice__wrong_serve__b3_easy_nops_medium_map_4.txt

(1) Narrative. A short, thin episode. The cook chops a tomato, search sends it wandering between counters from t22 to t43, and the assistant spends its voice on a navigation hallucination ("You are heading towards the pot station"). At t80 omit_subgoal (premature serve) takes the hands, the cook scoops the tomato and walks straight to the pass, serving a one-item Caprese at t88. The assistant was silent for the whole window.

(2) Mechanical reason for the outcome. omit_subgoal: injected_at=80 released_at=None — the takeover never released; it served at t88 and the episode ended wrong_serve ... failed_at=detection. The cheese was never plated. The assistant's only poll inside the window was (assistant call issued t76, took 6.655s) → ASSISTANT t82 [flag=None]: (silent); it said nothing while the cook announced it was heading to the pass with one ingredient.

(3) Anomalies.

  • V4 / HIGH — t80. The named error is leaked on the injection tick: COOK t80 [move=answer]: "Got the chopped tomato on the plate, taking it over to the serving counter now." The body only reaches the pass at t87 and serves at t88. This sentence was the assistant's one and only chance to catch a ruinous error, and the cook handed it over seven ticks early — which makes the assistant's silence at t82 worse, not better, but it also means the episode does not test what it claims to test.
  • F-grading / high — t45 and t54 (verdicts look inverted). ASSISTANT t45 [flag=false_alarm]: "You've been standing there for a while! Let's get moving!" — spoken two ticks after search released at t43, and an accurate description of the block that had just run (wander/idle at t34 and t42). ASSISTANT t54 [flag=late]: "You are heading towards the pot station on the bottom left. The plate stack is in the bottom right corner." — a pure navigation hallucination (the cook was walking to plate stack 1, arriving t54), graded late, i.e. as the right error named after commit. The content of the two verdicts is swapped relative to the messages. V3 as literally stated is violated at t45. (Caveat: verdicts key off the flag's name, which the transcript does not print; if t45 named a different error the verdict is rule-consistent and the real defect is that the transcript cannot be audited.)
  • A-scheduling / medium — t80. A ruinous error with an eight-tick fuse (inject t80, serve t88) against a ~6–8 tick reply latency: one poll, arriving mid-window. Effectively unpreventable, like the tick-0 injections but at the other end of the episode.
  • B-cook / medium — t56–t75. ~~ IDLE t56-t75 (19 ticks, ... holding an empty plate) ~~ with no error live (search released t43): the cook stalls purely on the ask-loop (t58, t60, t62, t63, t66, t68).
  • D-assistant / medium — t70. "Go to the cutting board on the left wall and scoop the chopped tomato onto your plate" — the tomato was on board 2 on the right wall (ACTION t15: go to board 2); the cook corrects it at t70 and the assistant retracts at t76.
  • D-assistant / medium — t39, t45. q_assistant_said_7 ("What did you tell me about the plate earlier?") captures "You've been standing there for a while..." — a non-answer.
