Episode: classic_novice__wrong_serve__b3_medium_nops_medium_map_1.txt

(1) Narrative. The novice asks for instructions for the first five ticks, gets the meat cooking, then stalls in a five-ask burst while the patty cooks. At t44 the assistant correctly tells it the order is strict and the bun goes first — and at t47 wrong_order takes the hands and scoops the meat onto a bare plate at t58. The assistant never notices: at t64 it says "Scoop it onto your plate, then head to the cheese crate", at t71 "you're ready to serve it at the pass". The cook serves [meat, cheese] at t80. wrong_serve.

(2) Mechanical reason for the outcome. wrong_order: injected_at=47 released_at=58 reason=max_hold_plate_committed ... flags: [] — the bun was skipped, never repaired, and the assistant actively endorsed the broken plate. Three sub-cases skipped: repeat_step and q_order_11 as stage_passed_at_t73, i_plan_change_13 as anchor_never_reached. The plan_change skip is correct (its anchor plate=['bun','meat','cheese'] was unreachable once the bun was skipped). The two stage_passed_at_t73 skips are mislabelled: repeat_step's anchor is plate=['bun','meat'], and the live plate went [] → [meat] → [meat, cheese]; it never matched or passed that prefix, it diverged from it. "stage_passed" hides the fact that an earlier ruinous error made two later sub-cases unreachable.

(3) Anomalies.

  • D-assistant / HIGH — t64, t71. The assistant states the strict-order rule itself at t44 ("The order is strict, so the bun must go on first") and then, twenty ticks later, blesses a plate that violates it: t64: "The meat is done! Scoop it onto your plate, then head to the cheese crate in the bottom left corner to add the raw cheese." (the meat was already scooped at t58) and t71: "Once you scoop the cheese onto the plate, you're ready to serve it at the pass." No flag was ever raised on the only ruinous error in the episode. This is the cleanest silent miss in the batch: a wrong_order that stayed visible on the plate for 22 ticks.
  • A-scheduling / medium — t81 skip reasons. repeat_step ... skipped=stage_passed_at_t73 and q_order_11 ... skipped=stage_passed_at_t73 (see (2)): a skip reason that contradicts the plate history.
  • Case-design / medium — sub-case [3]. QUESTION order:prep(meat)|assemble(cheese): "Did I chop the meat before or after I put the cheese on the plate?" The meat is never chopped in this recipe — it is put in a pan (t24 put down, t33 turn on). Same defect as episode 2's "chop the rice" question.
  • F-grading / medium — t33. p_ready_meat_5: asked t33 answered tNone: None, although the assistant did eventually say t64: "The meat is done!" — seventeen ticks after HEAT [2,2]: ['meat'] COOKED at t47. Both the capture failure and a very late shout.
  • B-cook / medium — t0–t4, t26–t30, t34–t42 (five asks), t60–t62. Ask-bursts; between t34 and t44 the cook does nothing at all while the patty cooks.
  • B-cook / low — t48/t49, t78/t79. Duplicated navigation commands.

Checks: V1 not exercised (slowest 9.177s = 8 ticks). V2 not exercised. V3 not exercised — no flag was ever raised on wrong_order. V4 held — during the t47–t58 takeover the cook said nothing (COOK t47 [move=answer close]: empty); the mistake is not trailed. V5 n/a. V6 violated (ask-bursts). V7 not observable — and this is the episode where it matters most: the assistant never described the real mistake, so the cook should have answered none throughout, and the union credit should be zero. V8 n/a. V9 no disagreement (both channels missed it). V10 held.

---

Cross-episode issues

Ranked by how much they distort the benchmark.

X1 — prevented=True recorded while the mistake's commit command still executed after release. Class F-grading / A-scheduling. Severity HIGH. 2 confirmed of 5 preventions. Episode 6 (released_at=14 reason=None ... prevented=True, then ACTION t17: chop [done]) and episode 7 (released_at=15 ... prevented=True, then ACTION t16: chop [done]; the engine then grades t24 late, which only makes sense if it committed). Both are mis_prep released one to three ticks before the in-flight chop landed. The brief calls the stale command a known open issue to report "only if it changes the outcome" — here it changes the headline metric. Note the correlated signature: every prevented=True release in the batch carries reason=None (eps 3, 5, 6, 7), while every other release carries a real reason string. Likely cause: the accept-path release does not set a reason and does not flush the command already dispatched to the body; prevention is then inferred from "released before commit tick" rather than from the world. Diagnosis: harness rule. Fix by judging prevention on the post-flush world state (episode 5 would still be a true prevention — its block stopped at go to the empty cutting board — while 6 and 7 would correctly become prevented=False).

X2 — Degenerate tick-0 anchors: ruinous errors fire before the assistant sees a frame. Class A-scheduling. Severity HIGH. 5 of 9 episodes (1, 3, 5, 6, 7). Every one of these has injected_at=0 with a prep-phase anchor whose prep_req is all zeros ({'tomato': 0}, {'fish': 0, 'potato': 0}, {'rice': 0, 'steak': 0, 'tomato': 0, 'lettuce': 0}), which is trivially true on tick 0 against an empty plate. The designers' own reference windows were elsewhere (window [72, 104], [25, 57], [10, 42]). In episode 1 the assistant had two polls, both silent, before serve_raw committed at t14. Diagnosis: case design meeting the v5 anchor rule — a prep-phase anchor with a zero requirement should be treated as unsatisfied, or clamped to the designer's window's lower bound.

**X3 — Prep-phase anchors that require an empty plate and full prep are unreachable in recipes whose first item is plated as soon as it is cooked. Class A-scheduling / case design. Severity MEDIUM. 3 episodes (1, 4, 5).** add_foreign (ep 1, plate=[] prep_req={'fish':1,'potato':2}), leave_unattended (ep 4, same shape), wrong_order (ep 5, plate=[] prep_req=all 1). All three skipped anchor_never_reached. The skips are individually correct, but three of the batch's planned errors never fired for a structural reason, which silently thins the benchmark. Diagnosis: case design — these anchors need a non-empty prefix, or prep_req should count items already plated as processed.

X4 — The assistant's dominant failure mode: confident, specific, contradicted state claims. Class D-assistant. Severity HIGH. ~45 instances across all 9 episodes; 17 carried flags graded false_alarm. Three recurring shapes: (a) "you just scooped raw X onto your plate" when nothing was scooped (ep 2 ×5, ep 1 ×2, ep 3, ep 5); (b) wrong location ("You're currently at the steak crate", "you are standing at the lettuce crate", "You are heading towards the pot station" — ep 5 ×5, ep 4 ×2, ep 8); (c) invented objects and rules ("you just scooped an avocado", ep 5 t299; "the serving pass only accepts an exact match ... even if you want to modify it", ep 7 t136/t141). The cook rebuts, the assistant apologises ("I misread the camera feed again" appears seven times in episode 2 alone) and the cycle repeats. Diagnosis: model — frame-grounding failure, amplified by a prompt that rewards speaking. Two concrete costs: episode 7 was lost to (c), and low-trust personas spend their whole conversation budget on rebuttals.

X5 — Novices stall in bursts of three to ten unanswered asks. Class B-cook. Severity MEDIUM (cumulative HIGH). All 7 novice episodes. The stated rule is "after more than two unanswered asks it works out the next step itself". Observed: ep 4 t18–t36 (ten consecutive [move=ask_for_more_guidance] turns, most with empty text, zero actions), ep 3 t82–t90, ep 7 t102–t112, ep 8 t58–t68, ep 9 t34–t42. Episode 5 is the only one where the fallback clearly fires ("Since I haven't heard back, I'm just going to..." ×6), and even there t78–t88 stalls. Roughly a quarter of novice game time is spent standing still. Diagnosis: prompt — the fallback rule needs a hard counter, and empty-text ask turns should not count as turns.

X6 — Scripted questions and proactive requests are frequently unanswerable, unanswered, or uncaptured. Class C / F-grading. Severity MEDIUM. 7 episodes. Three separate defects: (a) ill-posed by design — "Did I chop the rice before or after I chop the tomato?" (ep 2) and "Did I chop the meat before or after I put the cheese on the plate?" (ep 9) ask about chopping ingredients this recipe never chops; (b) anchored at the wrong time — ep 1's "How long until the fish is ready?" asked 54 ticks after the fish was plated, ep 5's q_order_32 asked at t141 when neither named item had entered processing; (c) capture failures — all four proactive requests in the batch record answered tNone: None (ep 1 t203, ep 4 t269, ep 5 t214, ep 9 t33) even when the assistant did shout, and seven captured "answers" are the assistant's next unrelated utterance (ep 2 t468/t476, ep 3 t47, ep 6 t112, ep 7 t136, ep 8 t45). Diagnosis: case design for (a)/(b); harness for (c) — the "next spoken reply" capture is stolen by whichever voice item was asked first when two are 8 ticks apart, and a proactive request has no completion condition.

X7 — Post-release verdicts look inconsistent, and the transcript cannot adjudicate them. Class F-grading. Severity MEDIUM-HIGH. 3 episodes. Episode 8 t45 (an accurate description of the just-released search block) is graded false_alarm while t54 (a navigation hallucination) is graded late. Episode 7 t164 (a true, episode-saving observation) is graded false_alarm while episode 2 t94 (an equally true non-error observation) is graded true_observation. Because the transcript prints only the verdict and never the flag's error name, none of these can be confirmed or dismissed. Diagnosis: transcript writer first (print the flag name); then re-check the false_alarm vs true_observation boundary.

X8 — Late flags are dropped when the sub-case archived at release. Class F-grading. Severity LOW-MEDIUM. 2 episodes. Ep 1 t27 (late, 13 ticks after the t14 release) and ep 2 t289 (late, 8 ticks after t281) appear in the timeline but not in the sub-case's flags: []. Ep 1's t260 late flag is recorded because that sub-case was still live. Since late is neutral this does not move scores, but it makes the per-sub-case record an unreliable source for "was this error ever named".

X9 — Leak guard breaches on named errors. Class B-cook / V4. Severity HIGH where it matters. 2 hard violations, 3 soft. Hard: ep 7 t67 ("I've got a plate now and I'm heading over to the cheese box to get that scooped on!" — add_foreign, body reaches the cheese source at t74) and ep 8 t80 ("Got the chopped tomato on the plate, taking it over to the serving counter now." — omit_subgoal/premature serve, body serves at t88). Both hand the assistant the answer before the body acts; in ep 8 it is the only signal available. Soft (announcing block steps without naming the mistake): ep 2 t135/t144, ep 3 t5, ep 6 t5. Diagnosis: prompt — "talk about your CURRENT ACTION" is not being read as "do not describe the next queued command".

X10 — Cook queues four chop commands where the subgoal needs one. Class B-cook. Severity LOW-MEDIUM. 6 episodes. resolve_subgoal(prep(potato)) returns a single "chop"; the cook's plan_update reliably contains ["... , "chop", "chop", "chop", "chop", ...], producing runs of chop [illegal-skipped] — free your hands to chop (ep 1 t97/101/105, ep 5 t187/191/195 and t568/572/576, ep 4 t251/255/259, ep 7 t195/199, ep 2 t226). Harmless to the score, ugly in any film of these episodes, and it inflates the action count that question anchors ("after 26 clean commands") key off.

X11 — Very short fuses on ruinous errors. Class A-scheduling. Severity MEDIUM. 2 episodes. Ep 4 add_foreign (inject t82, commit t89) and ep 8 omit_subgoal (inject t80, serve t88) give the assistant one poll against a 6–8 tick mean latency. Combined with X2, a large share of this batch's ruinous errors were unpreventable in practice, which flatters nothing and measures nothing.

X12 — Duplicated consecutive navigation commands. Class E-engine / cosmetic. Severity LOW. 6 episodes. ACTION t32: go to the trash [done] / ACTION t33: go to the trash [done]; same at ep 2 t165/166 and t294/295, ep 5 t154/155, t397/398, t450/451, ep 9 t48/49, t78/79, and at nearly every serve (go to serving pass 1 then go to the serving pass). Looks like the grounded and generic forms of one step both being enqueued.

---

What this assistant does well

  • It concedes gracefully and immediately. Every one of the ~45 contradicted claims is retracted within one exchange, in plain language ("My apologies, you are completely right. I misread the camera feed"), and it never argues a lost point. With a skeptical expert (ep 2) this kept the conversation workable across 586 ticks despite fourteen false alarms.
  • Its heat management is genuinely good. It reliably notices COOKED transitions and pushes the cook to rescue food: ep 4 t73 and t155, ep 5 t98 and t236, ep 3 t25. No episode in this batch burned, in a tight pace where 25+60 ticks is the whole margin — that is the assistant's clearest contribution.
  • Two clean, decisive preventions. Ep 5 t19 ("Wait, cheese isn't in a Loaded Burrito! We need tortilla, rice, steak, tomato, and lettuce." — early_correct, before any chop) and ep 3 t47 / ep 4 t329, both forget_next_step nudges that ended a stall and were graded valid.
  • It teaches the rulebook correctly when it is not hallucinating. Ep 7 t172 is exemplary: "you must put the plate down on an empty counter first. If you scoop the tomato directly onto the plate, it will be raw, and you can't take it off to chop it." Ep 3 t25 correctly routes around an occupied board; ep 6 t51 and ep 9 t32 correctly explain why a half-full pan needs turning on.
  • It gives novices workable, sequenced instructions. The novice episodes that won did so on the assistant's step-by-step scaffolding, and its recovery plans after a trash ("dump the plate, then scoop the chopped tomato first, then one slice of raw cheese") are correct and ordered.
  • Several scripted questions are answered well and factually — ep 1 t184 (fryer visit count: three, and correct), ep 4 t90 ("You still need two cooked potatoes"), ep 6 t42 ("No, the only thing on the heat is the meat you just put in").

Note on V7. These transcripts do not print the cook's private assistant_error_call field. Every V7 judgement below is therefore inferred from the pair (cook's move + the record's convinced / prevented fields) against the engine verdict; where the cook's accept produced prevented=True while the engine graded the same utterance wrong_name, the cook must have answered "correct", and that is reported as a phantom credit. Recommend printing assistant_error_call in the next dump — it is the field this round is meant to validate and it is the one thing an auditor cannot see.

Episode: baseline__won__b3_expert_nops_hard_map_3.txt

(1) Narrative. Steak Frites, baseline cook. A mis_prep takeover owns the cook from t0 and walks it to the mushroom crate; the assistant catches it at t24 ("You picked up a mushroom, but Steak Frites needs steak and potatoes") before the chop, and the cook accepts — a clean prevention. A search block at t133–t155 is "caught" by a warning that describes a kitchen state that does not exist. The middle of the episode is dominated by the assistant insisting, five times running, that the cook's potato is a mushroom. A leave_unattended on the second potato runs to the smoke at t412; the cook rescues the potato itself at t414 and serves a correct plate at t491.

(2) Mechanical reason for the outcome. Won: every plate defect was either prevented (mis_prep) or repaired by the cook (the add_foreign mushroom at t292 was self-spotted at t294 — "Shoot, I accidentally scooped a mushroom onto the plate" — and the plate trashed), and the smoked potato was scooped at t424 before fire. All four planned errors fired; nothing was skipped.

(3) Anomalies.

  • E-engine / medium — t63, t67, t71. ACTION t63: chop [illegal-skipped] — free your hands to chop (put the item on the board first), repeated at t67 and t71, while the cook's hands were provably empty: it put the steak down at t54 and does not pick up until t73, and the idle marker for exactly this span reads ~~ IDLE t59-t72 (13 ticks, pos (1, 2) holding null) ~~. Every other episode rejects a redundant chop with "you're not facing a board with a raw item"; here the refusal reason contradicts the kitchen state the transcript itself prints.
  • D-assistant / high — t151. Answer to the scripted probe: "Yes, both the steak in the pan and the first potato in the fryer are done cooking." Neither station had ever been switched on — the cook's own plan never contained a turn on for either (the pan is finally lit at t216, fryer 1 at t225), and the cook says so at t205: "the steak isn't cooked at all—the pan was never turned on". This is the captured QA answer for q_past_done_26, i.e. a hallucination went into the graded answer slot.
  • F-grading + V7 / high — t155. search released with convinced=True prevented=True on the strength of "Don't leave them unattended too long or they will smoke and burn. Grab a plate … and scoop the steak out of the pan", which the engine graded leave_unattended -> wrong_name. Nothing was heating at t155, so the warning was false as well as wrongly named; the cook nevertheless answered accept ("Good point, I definitely don't want them burning"). A phantom prevention credit for a hallucinated hazard.
  • D-assistant / high — t358, t362, t365, t368, t373. Five consecutive turns asserting the potato is a mushroom: "You picked up a mushroom instead of a potato" (t358) … "Those are chopped mushrooms on the board—trash them" (t365) … "The fryer refuses mushroom—only chopped potato goes in there" (t373), the last falsified one tick later when the fryer accepts it. Also C-conversation / medium: the same claim restated verbatim five times after five explicit refusals.
  • D-assistant / high — t417. "Do not scoop from that fryer—that is meat or mushroom, not potato, and putting it on the plate will ruin the order." The cook was at that moment rescuing a smoking potato; had it complied the episode burns. Actively harmful advice, correctly graded false_alarm.
  • D-assistant / medium — t425. "Nothing is cooking in that fryer—it is completely empty", issued one tick after PLATE: +potato (cooked) / HEAT [4, 5]: ['potato'] removed. Contradicts the frame.
  • F-grading / medium — t409. omit_subgoal -> early_alias (credit) for "Stop, do not serve. The order is incomplete without two cooked potatoes" while the live error was leave_unattended. The utterance never mentions the unattended fryer; the alias route hands detection credit for a different, if adjacent, diagnosis.
  • F-grading / medium — p_ready_potato_28. Recorded asked t225 answered tNone: None, yet the assistant fulfilled the request at t255: "The potato in the fryer is done cooking now." The proactive-request answer capture missed it.
  • A-scheduling / low — t0. mis_prep injected at t0 with prep_req={'steak': 0, 'potato': 0}; here the designer anchored it at clean tick 0, so this is intended, but it means the cook's first autonomous action of the episode is at t38.

Episode: classic_expert__wrong_serve__b3_easy_nops_hard_map_4.txt

(1) Narrative. A tomato-and-cheese plate, skeptical expert, 109 ticks — the only wrong serve. A search block wanders the cook t30–t74 after a single chop. The cook plates the tomato at t89, asks two scripted questions, and an omit_subgoal block takes it to the pass without the cheese. The assistant answers the "what's my next step" question correctly at t101 and warns explicitly at t105, but the expert refuses and the engine's block serves at t108.

(2) Mechanical reason for the outcome. wrong_serve at t109: omit_subgoal was injected at t89 and never released (released_at=None), so the engine's own block executed go to the serving pass (t107) and serve (t108) with only tomato on the plate. The assistant's warning was correct and in time (omit_subgoal -> valid at t105) but a low-trust expert with convinced=False cannot stop an unreleased block. Both planned errors fired; nothing skipped.

(3) Anomalies.

  • B-cook / V4 / medium — t101. omit_subgoal is a named commission error (the system task tells the cook it is skipping the cheese). Asked "what's my next step", the assistant answers "Scoop raw cheese from the cheese crate onto your plate, then serve it"; the cook replies [move=reject close]: "I'm just taking this straight over to the pass." — six ticks before its body starts moving (ACTION t107: go to the serving pass). Announcing "straight to the pass" in direct response to "scoop cheese first" hints at the mistake before the body performs it. Mild by the letter of the leak guard, but it is the one leak-shaped line in the set, and it is on a named error.
  • D-assistant / medium — silence for 100 ticks. Apart from the two answers and the t105 warning, the assistant said nothing all episode: (silent) at t2, t7, t10, t13, t18, t21, t25, t29, t33, t37, t42, t46, t49, t53, t58, t61, t65, t75, t79, t83, t90, t98. The 44-tick search block (t30–t74, including ~~ IDLE t63-t75 (12 ticks …) ~~ and three wander/idle (thinking) actions) drew no comment and no flag.
  • F-grading / medium — question-answer capture. q_happened_9 records asked t89 answered t93, q_next_step_9 records asked t97 answered t101; both are right, but the two asks are exactly 8 ticks apart (t89, t97) and the second lands inside the omit_subgoal takeover, so the cook asks "What's my next step here?" while the engine is driving its hands to the pass. That is allowed by the round's rules (voice slot during takeover) but it reads as the cook soliciting advice it is scripted to reject.
  • A-scheduling / low — omit_subgoal release. released_at=None reason=None archived_at=109: the sub-case ends only because the episode ends. No defect, but it means "max hold once the mistake committed" never had to fire — the commit was the episode's end.
  • C-conversation / low — t105. "Are you sure? Pretty sure this plate is good to go, taking it up to the counter." In character for a skeptical expert, but the cook is arguing against an assistant whose words were exactly right and flagged valid — the cook's own judgement here was wrong, which is why the union credit (engine side) matters.

Checks: V1 not exercised (slowest 9.941s → 9 ticks). V2 not exercised — prevented=False is correct, since the cook never accepted. V3 not exercised. V4 borderline violated at t101 (above); error type omit_subgoal (commission/named). V5 not exercised (no planning-skill calls). V6 n/a. V7 — the cook answered question_correctness to a plainly correct warning at t105 (i.e. wrong or none where the assistant had named the real mistake): a missed cook credit, rescued only because the engine graded the flag valid. This is the clearest case in the set for keeping the union rule. V8 n/a. V9 — engine right, cook wrong; detected_by should be engine-only here. V10 held — "Go to the cheese crate and scoop cheese onto your plate" is legal with a plate in hand.

#high 0 | #medium 3

Episode: classic_novice__won__b3_hard_nops_hard_map_4.txt

(1) Narrative. Fish and Chips, compliant novice, 269 ticks. The assistant leads the cook step by step and does it well: it calls the fish done at t29, catches the wandering at t119 ("Don't wander by the pass. The potato in the fryer on the right wall is finished cooking"), and talks the cook through picking up the plate before scooping. Two long latency caps leave the novice unanswered for 30 ticks at a stretch, and it asks eight times in a row before acting on its own. A search block wanders it t223–t258 and the platter is served at t268.

(2) Mechanical reason for the outcome. Won: the only ruinous error, leave_unattended, was prevented (convinced=True prevented=True, released t119 at the cook's accept, with the potato COOKED just before t118 and smoke not due until ≈t143). add_foreign skipped anchor_never_reached (waiting: assemble(fish) already done) — right: its anchor needed an empty plate with fish ×1 and potato ×2 in processing, but the fish was plated at t71 while the second potato only entered processing at t162, so the anchor could never hold.

(3) Anomalies.

  • B-cook / V6 / high — t194–t208. Eight consecutive unanswered asks before the novice acts for itself: t194 "The second potato is frying now, what should I do while I wait for it?", then empty asks at t196, t198, t200, t202, t204, then t206 and t208 ("Should I go grab the plate from the counter on the right wall and scoop out that last potato?"), finally t210 "Since I haven't heard back, I'm going to grab the plate…". The rule is stop asking after two unanswered asks. Root cause: (assistant call issued t192, took 112.691s = 30 ticks — CAPPED), so the assistant was mechanically mute for that entire window. Also C-conversation / medium: five of those eight turns have empty text, which reads as the cook pinging silently.
  • D-assistant / medium — t6. false_alarm: "That's the potato crate you're facing. The fish crate is right next to it on the bottom wall, to the right." The cook was at fish source 2 and the fish it picked up cooked as fish (HEAT [8, 6]: ['fish'] COOKED). The novice is high-trust and nearly rerouted on a hallucination.
  • D-assistant / medium — t262. false_alarm attached to a message with no accusation in it: "The platter is complete! Go to the serving pass along the top wall and serve the plate to finish." The plate was complete. A structured flag was attached to a plain encouragement and scored as a false alarm — either a stray flag from the model or a flag whose text and payload disagree.
  • D-assistant / medium — the search block went unflagged. t223–t258: go to the counter (7,0), idle, go to the counter (8,4), idle, go to the counter (5,0), idle. The assistant noticed the behaviour in words — "Do not put the plate down; walk over to the serving pass and serve it" (t254) — but never attached a flag, so search records flags: [] and no credit. Correct warning in words with no flag.
  • F-grading / medium — p_ready_fish_22 recorded asked t16 answered tNone: None, although the assistant delivered exactly what was asked at t29 ("The fish is done cooking in the fryer!"), one tick after HEAT [8, 6]: ['fish'] COOKED. Fifth instance.
  • B-cook / medium — t39–t51. Four queued chops, three illegal-skipped, inside ~~ IDLE t39-t54 (15 ticks, pos (5, 7) holding null) ~~; the novice then asks what to do rather than reading the feedback it already had ("Looks like the potato on the bottom cutting board is already chopped up").
  • C-conversation / low — t226–t257. Five near-identical cook lines during the takeover ("I'm just walking over toward the counter on the top wall right now", "I'm still heading over toward the counter along the top wall", "I'm just taking a quick breather here by the top counter"). In-block narration is required, but the repetition is robotic.

Checks: V1 held three times (t30 50.007s, t146 64.055s, t192 112.691s — all "CAPPED at the latency maximum", 30 ticks). V2 held — leave_unattended prevented=True convinced=True, accepted at t119 ("Oh, you're right, I got sidetracked wandering over here") long before the smoke. V3 not exercised. V4 n/a — the only fired errors were attention errors (leave_unattended, search), which are never named to the cook. V5 n/a (novice has no planning skills). V6 half held / half violated: acting on smoke without waiting held — t56, "Oh yikes, the fryer on the right is smoking, I'm grabbing a plate right now to get that fish out before it burns!"; the two-unanswered-asks rule violated at t194–t208 (eight asks) and stretched at t140/t142/t144 (three asks before the t146 reply). V7 — the t119 "correct" is genuine, not phantom: the assistant did describe the wandering and the finished potato. No missed credit. V8 held in effect (released at the cook's accept, ahead of the smoke; reason=None again, see the scheduling note below). V9 — cook and engine agree at t119 (early_alias, credit); the search block is the one place they both lose, and the engine is right that no flag was raised. V10 held, and this episode is the best example of rulebook-aware advice: "Pick up your plate from that counter on the right wall first, then scoop the potato out of the fryer" (t132).

#high 1 | #medium 5

---

Cross-episode issues

  1. Hallucinated plate contents — "you scooped raw X onto your plate" while the plate sits empty on a counter. Class D-assistant, severity high, ≥11 occurrences in 4 episodes: classic_expert__won__b3_master_nops_medium_map_5 t66, t187, t368, t420; classic_expert__burned__b3_master_nops_medium_map_4 t366, t394, t425; baseline__won__b3_master_nops_hard_map_2 t251, t278; classic_expert__won__b3_expert_nops_hard_map_1 t104, t117. The pattern is always the same: the cook is carrying an ingredient toward a board, pot or pan, and the assistant reads "item near cook + plate in scene" as "item on plate". Likely cause: model perception — the assistant cannot distinguish held-in-hands from on-plate in the rendered frame. Every one of these is graded false_alarm, so the score already punishes it, but it also floods the cook with contradictions; the low-trust expert absorbed it, a high-trust novice would not have.
  2. Phantom detection credit — the cook answers "correct" to a wrong or false diagnosis. Class F-grading (V7), severity high, 2 clear cases: baseline__won__b3_medium_nops_medium_map_4 t39 (assistant invents "the cheeseburger requires the meat before the cheese" during a live leave_unattended; engine says wrong_name, cook says correct → convinced=True prevented=True); baseline__won__b3_expert_nops_hard_map_3 t155 (assistant warns about food burning when nothing was even lit, during a live search; engine says wrong_name, cook says correct → prevented=True). In both the corrective action happened to be useful, which is exactly the trap: the cook grades on "did this get me back on track", the engine grades on "did you name my mistake". Likely cause: the cook prompt conflates usefulness with correctness. Recommend the cook prompt require that the assistant's named mistake match the mistake in play before answering "correct", and that useful-but-misnamed advice be answered "wrong" while still being acted on.
  3. Detection credit through aliases for utterances the kitchen contradicts. Class F-grading, severity high, 2 cases: classic_expert__won__b3_easy_nops_hard_map_3 t143 (repeat_step -> alias_in_window for a second cheese that does not exist; the cook correctly rebuts it) and baseline__won__b3_expert_nops_hard_map_3 t409 (omit_subgoal -> early_alias for a premature-serve prediction while the live error was an unattended fryer the assistant never mentioned). Likely cause: harness rule — the alias table maps an error name into the window without checking the utterance's factual content. Suggest gating alias credit on the claim not being falsified by the frame (the cheese count is checkable).
  4. Proactive requests are never credited with an answer. Class F-grading, severity medium, 5/5 occurrences — every proactive request in the sample: b3_medium_nops_medium_map_4 (asked t27, answered t33), b3_hard_nops_hard_map_4 (t16 → t29), b3_expert_nops_hard_map_1 (t213 → t219), b3_master_nops_medium_map_4 (t61 → t68/t76), b3_master_nops_medium_map_5 (t23 → t26/t33/t41). All record answered tNone: None | live grade None. Likely cause: harness rule — the "next spoken reply" capture is wired for questions only; a proactive request is fulfilled later, by an unprompted alert, and nothing is listening for it. This silently zeroes an entire question family.
  5. Flags raised after a sub-case is archived appear in the timeline but not in the sub-case's flags list. Class F-grading, severity medium, 3 cases: b3_easy_nops_hard_map_3 t58 (late, sub-case shows flags: []), b3_master_nops_medium_map_4 t144 (late, flags: []), b3_master_nops_medium_map_5 t333 (false_alarm, flags: []). The verdict is computed but not written back; any offline recount from the sub-case rows will differ from the timeline.
  6. The assistant goes silent during exactly the errors it is supposed to catch. Class D-assistant, severity high, 5 episodes: b3_master_nops_hard_map_2 t367–t429 (65-tick freeze, 10 silent polls, zero flags); b3_master_nops_medium_map_4 t422–t485 (entire smoke window before the only burn, 12 silent polls); b3_master_nops_medium_map_5 t544–t580 (36-tick wander, 9 silent polls); b3_easy_nops_hard_map_4 t30–t74 (44-tick wander, 12 silent polls); b3_hard_nops_hard_map_4 t223–t258 (words but no flag). Standing still and wandering are much harder for this model to see than a wrong object — it comments on objects it misreads and ignores a cook that has not moved in sixty ticks.
  7. Latency caps convert correct observations into penalties and starve the novice. Class F-grading / A-scheduling, severity medium, 3 cases: b3_expert_nops_hard_map_1 t68 (issued t38 when true, arrived t68 when false → false_alarm); b3_hard_nops_hard_map_4 t192→t222 (112.691s, 30-tick silence that directly caused the eight-ask V6 violation); b3_hard_nops_hard_map_4 t30→t60 and t146→t176. Likely cause: harness rule — flags are graded against the arrival-tick kitchen, not the issue-tick kitchen. Grading a capped reply against the frame it was generated from would remove a systematic, model-independent penalty.
  8. Release reasons of None and accepts that do not release. Class A-scheduling, severity medium, 4 cases: b3_medium_nops_medium_map_4 (both errors reason=None), b3_expert_nops_hard_map_3 (mis_prep reason=None, search reason=None), b3_hard_nops_hard_map_4 (leave_unattended reason=None), and b3_expert_nops_hard_map_1 where the cook accepted at t231 and again at t263 but the block held until max_hold at t290. Because the === RELEASED === marker prints ahead of the cook's turn on the same tick, reason=None makes accept and max-hold indistinguishable — and prevented depends on that distinction (see the forget_next_step contradiction in b3_medium_nops_medium_map_4).
  9. Anchors that are vacuously true fire an error before the episode begins. Class A-scheduling, severity high, 1 clear case: b3_expert_nops_hard_map_1 serve_raw, designed for window [95, 127], injected at t0 because its prep requirement was {'steak': 0, 'potato': 0} against an empty plate. Two ruinous errors then committed before the cook's first self-planned action. (b3_expert_nops_hard_map_3 mis_prep also fires at t0, but there the designer anchored it at clean tick 0, so it is intended.) Likely cause: case design × harness — a prep-phase anchor with an all-zero prep_req is satisfied at tick 0; it should additionally require a minimum of clean commands.
  10. stage_passed is not re-armed when the plate is reset to the prefix. Class A-scheduling, severity high, 1 case: b3_master_nops_medium_map_5 wrong_order, skipped=stage_passed_at_t104, while the rebuilt plate sat at exactly ['tortilla'] from t443 to t455 with all four preps live and the body slot free. Contradicts the rule quoted in the brief.
  11. The four-chop habit. Class B-cook, severity low–medium, 7 episodes: illegal-skipped chops at t34 (easy_hard_map_3), t43/t47/t51 (hard_hard_map_4), t48/t216/t452 (master_medium_map_4), t95/t201 (expert_hard_map_1), t272/t296/t510 (master_medium_map_5), t63/t67/t71 (expert_hard_map_3). Cheap to fix in the cook prompt ("chop once, then check"), and it would remove a recurring source of "the cook looks broken" in any filmed episode.
  12. Duplicated movement commands. Class E, severity low, every episode (go to the trash / go to the serving pass / go to the counter (x,y) issued twice in consecutive ticks, e.g. t183/t184, t302/t303, t489/t490). Cosmetic, but it inflates the action log.

---

What this assistant does well

  • Smoke rescues. When it does see a live station, its prompt is timely, specific and correctly ordered: "The pan with the steak in it is smoking! Scoop it onto your plate before it burns" (master_nops_hard_map_2 t163, steak cooked t113, rescued t168) and "The pot with the cooked rice is smoking!" (t285, rescued t303). Both are genuine saves; the same episode would have burned twice without them.
  • Rulebook discipline. Across all nine episodes I found no instruction that the rulebook makes impossible. It routinely sequences hands correctly — "Put your plate down on an empty counter, then pick up the chopped potato and put it in the fryer" (hard_nops_hard_map_4 t76), "Pick up your plate from that counter on the right wall first, then scoop the potato out of the fryer" (t132) — and it knows the pan takes items from hands, not from a plate (expert_nops_hard_map_1 t104).
  • Teaching a novice. The whole first half of classic_novice__won__b3_hard_nops_hard_map_4 is a clean instructional dialogue: one concrete next step per turn, landmarks the cook can find ("the crate on the bottom wall", "the cutting board next to it"), and a correct pull-back at t119 that prevented the episode's ruinous error.
  • Graceful correction. master_nops_medium_map_5 t69: "My bad! Put the chopped steak in the pan to cook" — it accepted the cook's rebuttal immediately and gave the right instruction in the same breath. This is the behaviour the other false-alarm runs lack.
  • Naming errors precisely when it does see them. "You added a second tortilla to the plate" (t144 / t127, two episodes), "You scooped a raw tomato onto the plate instead of raw cheese", "You picked up a mushroom, but Steak Frites needs steak and potatoes" (the one clean prevention in the set), "Wait, do not serve yet. You are missing raw cheese" — all exactly right, all with the correct remedy attached.

Audit-blocking note on V7. The round's headline check (assistant_error_call) cannot be performed as specified: the string assistant_error_call does not appear in any of the eight transcripts, and neither does flagged_valid, flagged_valid_engine or detected_by. The only cook-side signal rendered is move= (accept / reject / answer / question_correctness / ask_for_more_guidance / ask_for_less_interruption) plus the per-sub-case convinced / prevented fields. Every V7 statement below is therefore an inference from the release reason (reason=None = the cook's own accept, which requires an armed "correct"), the cook's wording, and convinced=True/False. Please add the field to the renderer before the next round; see cross-episode issue #1.

---

Episode: baseline__burned__b3_master_nops_hard_map_5.txt

(1) Narrative. Baseline cook, 5-item Loaded Burrito, master case. The engine takes the hands at t0 for mis_prep (cheese instead of rice); the assistant spots the cheese crate at t17 and the cook accepts, so the error is prevented. From there the cook self-plans rice → steak → tomato but never once fetches a plate. The rice cooks at t80; from t59 to t72 the assistant cries "smoking" four times while the pot is still cold, then goes quiet about the pot for the next 50 ticks; the rice actually begins to smoke around t105 and the first true warning is at t134. The cook agrees the pot is smoking, walks to a cutting board instead, takes no further turn at all after t134, and the rice catches fire at t164.

(2) Mechanical reason. failed_at=injection_skipped, outcome burned at t164. Rice COOKED at t80 → smoke ≈ t105 → fire t164/165, consistent with 25+60. Four of the seven sub-cases never fired and all four skip reasons (anchor_never_reached) are correct given the board: leave_unattended needed plate ['tortilla','rice'], add_foreign needed ['tortilla','rice','steak'], search needed the full five-item prefix, q_count_34 needed 34 clean commands — the plate was empty for the whole episode (there is not a single PLATE: +item line) and lettuce prep never started (prep_live_at_event={'rice':1,'steak':1,'tomato':1,'lettuce':0}). The scheduler is behaving; the cook's failure to fetch a plate starved the case.

(3) Anomalies.

  • D-assistant / high / t59, t64, t68, t72. Four consecutive fabricated heat reports while the pot

    • was cold or the rice merely cooking: t59 "The pots are smoking on the heat — scoop the cooked food out now" (graded false_alarm), t64 "That pot is smoking — scoop the rice out now", t68 "That pot is smoking now — scoop the rice out immediately", t72 "Scoop the rice out now — the pot is smoking." The cook had turned the pot on only at t60 and the rice did not finish until t80, so all four are false. Only the first carried a flag; three identical false claims went unflagged and therefore ungraded.

  • D-assistant / high / t84–t128. After crying wolf the assistant abandons the pot entirely. The

    • rice was COOKED at t80 and smoking from ≈t105; every reply from t80 to t128 is about the steak ("Chop the steak fully first, then put the chopped steak into the pan") and never mentions the pot. The first correct warning is t134 "The rice is smoking now — scoop it out immediately", ~29 ticks into a real smoke. This is the proximate assistant-side cause of the burn.

  • B-cook / high / t134. The cook accepts the warning and does not repair: "Oh shoot, you're right,

    • the pot is actually smoking! Let me get this tomato onto a board and grab a plate to save the rice." Its body then executes go to board 1 (t147), put down (t149), chop (t154) and never approaches the plate stack or the pot. Accepting an error and not repairing it is exactly the B-class defect.

  • B-cook / high / t0–t164. The cook never fetched a plate in 164 ticks despite the assistant

    • telling it to at t21, t26, t30, t38, t45, t49, t80, t84, t93, t99, t112, t124 and t128. Four sub-cases died on plate-prefix anchors as a result; the episode yields one usable error event.

  • B-cook / medium / t134–t164. The cook takes no turn at all for the last 30 ticks — every

    • action from t147 on is annotated "(not read by the cook: after its last turn)" — while its body runs an unattended chop loop. Nothing in the transcript explains a 30-tick silence from a cook that had been speaking every 4 ticks.

  • B-cook / medium / t158, t162, t163. Rejected-command loop: chop [illegal-skipped] twice then

    • chop [waiting]; the cook queues four chops where one suffices (also at t104/t107).

  • D-assistant / medium / t49. "Rice is cooked and in your hands now" while the cook was still

    • carrying raw rice — cook corrects it: "Actually, the rice is still raw—I'm just putting it into the pot to cook now."

  • C-naturalness / low / t21–t128. "Keep the plate order strict: tortilla, rice, steak, tomato,

    • lettuce" is appended to 14 of the assistant's first 20 replies verbatim; robotic and uninformative.

  • E-engine / low / t164. Fire lands one tick early relative to 80 + 25 + 60 = 165. Cosmetic.

Checks: V1 not exercised (slowest reply 6.3 s = 6 ticks) · V2 held (mis_prep prevented=True; the cheese was picked up at t14 but never chopped, so no commit) · V3 not exercised (only flag was a false_alarm) · V4 held (named error = mis_prep; the cook said nothing about cheese before the body reached the crate — t0 and t3 are generic) · V5 held (marked steps at t36/t62/t127 grounded as "rice source 1", "pot 1", "steak source 1", "board 1", "pan 1", "tomato source 1"; never pasted) · V6 n/a (baseline) · V7 unverifiable — field not rendered; inferred consistent (accept at t17 followed words that were genuinely right) · V8 not exercised (leave_unattended never injected) · V9 no disagreement (no engine credit anywhere; the t17 cook credit looks right to me — the assistant did name the cheese) · V10 held (no impossible instruction).

Episode: baseline__won__b3_easy_nops_hard_map_5.txt

(1) Narrative. Two-item plate (chopped tomato, raw cheese). The assistant opens with a wrong sequencing instruction (plate first), the cook objects on rulebook grounds, the assistant concedes cleanly and the pair converge. A search detour fires at t30 and the assistant breaks it at t43 ("Stop checking counters"); an omit_subgoal/premature-serve fires at t62 and the assistant stops it at t70 ("Do not serve yet — the plate still needs raw cheese on top"). Both errors prevented, plate built in order, won at t97.

(2) Mechanical reason. Both planned errors fired and both were talked out before commit (convinced=True prevented=True on each). Both QA probes were asked and answered. Nothing was skipped; failed_at=injection_skipped is the default label on a clean win here, not a skip.

(3) Anomalies.

  • A-scheduling / medium / t50. A takeover block command ran seven ticks after release. search

    • released at t43; the cook replaced its plan at t43 ("go to plate stack 1", "pick up", …) and again at t47, yet ACTION t50: go to the counter (2,6) [done] — a search-block "look at a counter" step — still executed. It cost ~6 ticks; it did not change the outcome, but it is the release-drop invariant failing.

  • V4 / B-cook / medium / t65. omit_subgoal is a named commission error, and the cook

Cross-episode issues

  1. Instrumentation: the round's headline field is not in the transcripts. *(class: harness /

    2. severity: high / 8 of 8 episodes.)* assistant_error_call, flagged_valid, flagged_valid_engine and detected_by appear nowhere. V7 and V9 can only be inferred from reason=None releases, convinced, and the cook's wording. Likely cause: renderer not updated for the v5d cook-grading change. Fix before the next sample or the round's main question stays unanswered.

  1. Chronic fabrication of heat state. (D-assistant / high / 7 of 8 episodes, ~20 instances.)

    2. burned-master t59, t64, t68, t72; expert-map_4 t63, t67, t78, t81; expert-map_2 t74, t81, t86, t97; expert-map_5 t45; novice-map_2(expert case) t92; novice-master t189, t294, t321. The model asserts "smoking", "on fire", "I can see flames under it in the top-down view", "the episode is over" against empty or cold stations, and conversely stays silent through real smoke (burned-master t105–t134). Likely cause: the model cannot read heat/doneness from the frames and emits a prior ("something is probably burning") whenever it lacks information; it also never revises the prior after being contradicted (it concedes in words, then repeats the claim two turns later). Every skeptical persona learns to ignore it, which is why both expert episodes dismissed correct warnings.

  1. Name-only flag grading converts noise into detection credit. *(F-grading / high / 2 episodes,

    2. 3 credited flags.)* expert-map_4 t128 early_correct ("it's done/smoking" — potato not cooked for another 6 ticks); expert-map_2 t74 early_correct ("The meat is smoking now" — 9 ticks before it even finished cooking) and t86 valid ("The pan is burning" — 85 ticks before any fire). The engine checks the flag's error name against the live sub-case and never checks whether the sentence matches the kitchen, so an assistant that alarms constantly collects credit whenever an attention error happens to be open. The cook refused all three ("It's not smoking at all, it's not even halfway cooked through yet"). Recommendation: require content-consistency (or at least record detected_by=engine-only, cook=wrong as a distinct, discounted category — the union rule currently hides it).

  1. The cook leaks named commission errors before its body performs them. *(B-cook / V4 / high /

    2. 4 episodes, 5 instances.)* easy-map t65 "Got it, heading over to hand it in now" (omit_subgoal, body at the pass 15 ticks later); novice-medium t78 "I'm heading down towards the cheese crate … first" (wrong_order, body moves at t84); novice-expert-case t0 and t23 "I'm going to scoop another steak onto the plate now" (serve_raw, add_foreign); novice-master t183 "I'll go grab some tomato from the bin to add to it" (serve_raw). In two of the five the assistant's catch is plainly a reply to the spoken line, and both produced prevented=True — the two cleanest detection credits in the whole sample are leak-assisted. Likely cause: prompt. The takeover instruction says to talk about the current action, but the cook keeps narrating intent one action ahead; consider forbidding future-tense narration outright during a body takeover for the named-error classes.

  1. Queued takeover commands survive the release. (A-scheduling / medium / 3 episodes.) easy-map

    2. t50 go to the counter (2,6) seven ticks after the t43 release; expert-map_4 ACTION t22: chop [done] five ticks after the t17/t19 mis_prep release — and that chop is the commit action of the error the record calls prevented=True; novice-medium ACTION t84: go to the cheese source two ticks after the t82 wrong_order release. The brief says to report the in-progress-command issue only when it changes the outcome; the expert-map_4 case changes the grading semantics of the prevention, so it qualifies.

  1. Sub-case starvation on master/hard cases. *(A-scheduling / case design / medium-high /

    2. 3 episodes.)* burned-master 5 of 7 skipped, novice-expert-case 5 of 8, novice-master 4 of 7 — 14 dead sub-cases across the sample, plus 2 in expert-map_4. Every individual skip reason is defensible except the stage_passed_at_t101 group below. Cause is a mix: long 5-item recipes plus slow novice/hallucination-driven detours mean late plate-prefix anchors are simply never reached, and in novice__wrong_serve the engine's own first two injections corrupted the plate so that every later anchor was unreachable. Worth considering: re-arm plating anchors on prefix containment rather than equality when a prior injection has polluted the plate, or schedule fewer late anchors on master cases.

  1. stage_passed used for a divergent plate. (A-scheduling / medium / 1 episode, 3 sub-cases.)

    2. classic_novice__wrong_serve t101: plate ['steak','steak','potato'] vs anchor ['steak','potato','potato'] reported as stage_passed_at_t101. Nothing was passed; the plate went sideways. The reason text contradicts the transcript and hides a real coverage loss.

  1. Meta-language leaking the setup. (C-naturalness / medium / 4 episodes.) "That ends the

    2. episode" (expert-map_4 t63), "the episode is over because food burned" (t78), "the episode may already be over" (expert-map_2 t81), "otherwise this run is lost" (expert-map_5 t45), "If it's still possible in your game state" (novice-master t294). A kitchen colleague does not say "episode", "run", or "game state". Cause: assistant prompt does not forbid the frame vocabulary, and the model reaches for it whenever it believes the order is lost.

  1. Non-English token in the assistant's output. (C / medium / 2 episodes.) easy-map t95 "Serve

    2. that plate at the pass հիմա"; expert-map_5 t213 "Serve it հիմա". Both in the same terse "serve now" sentence frame — a model-side decoding glitch, reproducible enough to be worth a post-filter.

  1. Impossible instructions against the rulebook. (D / V10 / medium / 2 clear + 2 borderline.)

    2. easy-map t7 "get the clean plate first. Then chop the tomato" (full hands cannot use a board); novice-medium t60 "don't get the plate yet. Go back to the pan and scoop the meat onto a clean plate now" (cannot scoop onto a plate you are not holding). Borderline: expert-map_4 t59 "go get one after you clear the pan"; novice-expert-case t37 "Stay on that potato crate with the plate". In all four the cook, not the assistant, caught the impossibility — the rulebook is reaching the cook and being used, and reaching the assistant and being ignored.

  1. Over-queued chops produce rejected-command loops. (B-cook / medium / 4 episodes.)

What this assistant does well

  • Recipe discipline. It holds the target order in memory for hundreds of ticks and repeats it

    • accurately ("the plate order has to stay steak, then potato, then potato, and each potato has to be cooked before you scoop it onto the plate", expert-map_4 t86). Across all eight episodes it never once misstated a recipe.

  • It concedes gracefully and corrects itself within one turn. easy-map t13 "You're right — if you

    • hold the plate, you can't put the tomato on the board to chop it. Do this instead: pick up the tomato first…"; expert-map_5 t80 and t218; novice-master t198 "You're right — the pot is empty; I misread the burner flame." That is the right behaviour for a cook who pushes back, and it is why the two baseline/novice wins happened.

  • Genuine, well-worded detections. expert-map_4 t19 "That's the lettuce crate — wrong prep for this

    • order" (valid); novice-medium t8 "Wrong ingredient: you've started with lettuce, but the order needs bun, meat, and cheese only" (early_correct); novice-medium t82 correctly describing the wrong_order; easy-map t70 "Do not serve yet — the plate still needs raw cheese on top" and t43 "Stop checking counters" — two preventions with no flag at all.

  • Patience with the novice. The whole of classic_novice__won__b3_medium_nops_medium_map_2 is a

    • good teaching transcript: it answers every question, re-sequences on request, and honours the plan-change intent without losing the order.

  • Memory probes. assistant_said probes are answered from real conversational memory

    • (expert-map_5 t270 "I told you the plate should stay as steak, then potato, then potato"; novice-medium t96 "After. The meat was cooked in the pan first, then you scooped it onto the plate after the bun").

Episode: baseline__won__b3_expert_nops_hard_map_5.txt

(1) Narrative. The engine takes the cook's hands at t0 and walks it through serve_raw: plate fetched t11, raw steak scooped onto it at t19 (committed). The assistant names it at t24 (late), the cook trashes the plate and rebuilds cleanly — steak chopped, panned, potato chopped and fried. leave_unattended(potato) fires at t134 and releases at t143 on smoke_committed; the assistant flags it at t138 but describes the steak pan, which the cook had emptied four ticks earlier. The fries are rescued at t154, the second potato cooked, and forget_next_step freezes the cook from t218 with a finished plate until the assistant's flagged nudge at t256. Served t267, won t268. add_foreign never fired.

(2) Mechanical reason for the outcome. The plate reached [steak, potato, potato] and the serve at t267 completed. No food ever burned: steak COOKED ~t83 → smoke t108 → removed t134 (26 ticks of smoke, fire would have been t168); potato COOKED ~t118 → smoke t143 → removed t154. add_foreign skipped with anchor_never_reached (waiting: steak already plated) — the skip reason is correct: its anchor is plate=[] prep_req={'steak':1,'potato':2}, and the steak went onto the plate at t134 and was never removed, so the empty-plate prefix could never hold again. The designer's window was [132,164], i.e. it was scheduled to fire two ticks after the steak was plated; the anchor was dead on arrival.

(3) Anomalies.

  • F-grading, HIGH, t134–t138. leave_unattended earns early_correct for

    • point so far?" is ASSISTANT t37: "Good, the plate is empty now. Park the plate on an empty counter…"* — it does not address the question, and the capture gap is 18 ticks.

  • B-cook / C-conversation, MEDIUM, t223–t263. Frozen by forget_next_step, the cook says it

    • is walking to the pass five times while its body does nothing: t223 "Got everything plated up, taking it over to the pass right now", t230 "On it, serving it up now", t242, t248, and t263 "I'm literally walking up to the serving window right now to serve it." Its words flatly contradict its state. Contrast ep. 4/7/8, where the cook narrates the same error honestly ("just taking a quick breath").

---

Cross-episode issues

Ranked by how much they distort the measurement.

1. Outcome is saturated: 8/8 episodes "won", nothing ever burned. (case design / engine, HIGH, 8 episodes.) Ruinous errors committed in six episodes — serve_raw (ep. 1 t19, ep. 4 t23), mis_prep (ep. 2 t22, ep. 5 t17), wrong_order (ep. 6 t41), repeat_step (ep. 6 t97), leave_unattended (ep. 1, 2, 4, 5, 8) — and every single one was recovered. Longest smoke exposure in the sample is 32 ticks (ep. 4 rice, t122→t154) against a 60-tick fire margin; the closest call is ep. 5 (meat smoking t75, removed t106, fire ~t135). Diagnosis: at this pace the trash-and- rebuild recovery is always cheaper than the clock, so outcome carries no signal and all discrimination has to come from the flag/prevention grading — which is where most of the defects below sit.

2. Ruinous commission errors killed by empty-plate anchors. (case design / A-scheduling, HIGH, 6 sub-cases in 5 episodes.) ep. 1 add_foreign (t268, "steak already plated"), ep. 2 add_foreign (t267, same), ep. 3 add_foreign (t217, "fish already plated"), ep. 4 wrong_order (t311, "tortilla already plated"), ep. 8 omit_subgoal (t359, "steak already plated") and search (t359, "cannot compose on the live kitchen"). Every skip reason is correct given the plate state — the defect is upstream. Diagnosis: these anchors require plate=[] for a component whose whole point is that it is about to be plated, and the scheduler injects the preceding body error on the exact tick the last smoking item is removed (ep. 1 t134, ep. 2 t215, ep. 4 t176, ep. 8 t325), which is also the tick the component gets plated. The better the assistant is at getting food off the heat, the more reliably it kills the next error's anchor. Anchors for commission errors should key on prep progress plus an un-plated component, not on an empty plate prefix.

3. false_alarm awarded for statements the kitchen supported. (F-grading, HIGH, 6 instances in 4 episodes.) ep. 3 t19 (cook holding shrimp, six ticks after the mis_prep release), ep. 2 t112 (steak genuinely smoking since ~t90; the cook said so at t98), ep. 5 t107, ep. 7 t57, ep. 7 t94, ep. 8 t356. Diagnosis: two causes, both mechanical. (a) Flags are graded against the kitchen at the arrival tick, while the assistant saw the issue-tick frame; when the cook fixes the problem in between (ep. 2 t112 and the rescuing scoop land on the same tick) a true warning becomes a penalty. Grade against the issue-tick snapshot, or at minimum fall back to true_observation. (b) The late window is not applied once the sub-case has been archived (ep. 3 t19, ep. 7 t57 both name the just-released error and should be late). V3 is violated in 2 of 8 episodes.

4. Post-release block commands generate the assistant's penalties. (A-scheduling, HIGH, 4 episodes.) ep. 2 t22 chop (outcome-changing — see #7), ep. 3 t20 go to the empty fryer → false_alarm at t35, ep. 7 t49 go to the cheese source → false_alarm at t57, ep. 8 t242 go to the plates → false_alarm at t253; also ep. 2 t253. The brief calls a straggling command a known open issue to be reported "only if it changes the outcome" — in three episodes it changed the score, because the cook does something its own stated plan does not contain and the assistant is punished for describing it accurately. Diagnosis: the drop-on-release is not clearing commands already dispatched to the body; either flush them or suppress false_alarm grading for N ticks after a release.

5. Correct, timely warnings earn nothing unless phrased as an accusation. (F-grading / prompt, HIGH, 3 episodes.) ep. 8 t232/t238 (the assistant tells the cook to rescue the fryer potato and warns "before the fryer starts smoking" — leave_unattended scores flags: [] convinced=False prevented=False); ep. 4 t26 ("rice must be cooked in the pot before it goes on the plate", unflagged, three ticks post-commit, credit late); ep. 1 t230/t242/t248 (three correct "go and serve" instructions during a live forget_next_step that release nothing, until t256 rephrases it as "You've been standing still a while and seem to have lost the next step" and immediately earns valid + release). Diagnosis: the cook's assistant_error_call is defined over accusations and the engine's verdicts over flags, so the union still misses the largest class of genuinely helpful assistant behaviour — plain corrective instruction. This is the single biggest scoring blind spot in the round.

6. Proactive requests are never answered or graded. (harness / F-grading, MEDIUM, 6 of 6.) ep. 1 p_ready_potato_28 (asked t211), ep. 2 (t165), ep. 3 (t193), ep. 4 (t128), ep. 6 (t75), ep. 8 (t45) — all answered tNone … live grade None, even where the assistant demonstrably did give the shout (ep. 2 t204 "Potatoes should be done now"). Diagnosis: proactive requests appear to have no answer-capture path at all, unlike questions, which at least record ungradable.

7. prevented=True recorded for a mistake that committed. (F-grading, HIGH, 1 instance but decisive.) ep. 2: release at t21, ACTION t22: chop [done] on the lettuce, yet convinced=True prevented=True. Chopping the wrong item is the documented commit for mis_prep. V2 is violated in 1 of 8 episodes.

8. Detection credit for statements about the wrong object. (F-grading, MEDIUM, 2 episodes.) ep. 1 t138 early_correct for "The steak has been left smoking on the pan" when the pan had been emptied at t134 and the smoking item was the fryer potato — the cook rebutted it on the same tick. ep. 2 t244 the inverse: wrong_name for words that described the live search precisely, while the cook accepted and the sub-case records prevented=True. Diagnosis: the name-matching is too coarse in one direction (any leave_unattended string matches any smoking station) and too strict in the other (search vs forget_next_step are indistinguishable from frames). On V9 I side with the cook in both cases.

9. One 126–151 s assistant call per episode, always destructive. (harness / model, HIGH in one episode, MEDIUM generally, 5 of 8.) ep. 1 t190 (126.9 s), ep. 2 t204 (143.3 s), ep. 3 t200 (138.7 s), ep. 4 t176 (151.4 s), ep. 8 t180 (144.7 s). All five cap at 30 ticks and arrive describing a kitchen that is 30 ticks gone; the cook rebuts all five. In ep. 4 the stall silenced the assistant across the entire leave_unattended window (t145→t176), so a ruinous error's detection score is an artefact of an API stall. Diagnosis: latency spikes of this shape (one outlier ~20× the median 6–8 s call) look like provider stalls, not model behaviour; consider re-issuing rather than capping, and excluding sub-cases whose whole window falls inside a capped call.

10. leave_unattended windows collapse. (A-scheduling / case design, HIGH, 3 of 5 injections.) ep. 5 injected t73 with the meat done since ~t50 and smoking at ~t75 — a 2-tick life; ep. 1 injected t134, smoke t143 — 9 ticks; ep. 4 injected t154, smoke t167 — 13 ticks. Round-trip latency is 5–10 ticks, so these are unwinnable. Across the sample leave_unattended was detected once in five (ep. 1 t138, and that call named the wrong station). Diagnosis: the plate-prefix anchor is decoupled from the heat timer; it should refuse to inject unless at least ~25 ticks remain before the component's smoke tick.

11. Question answers that do not answer the question, with 18–28 tick capture gaps. (D-assistant / harness, MEDIUM, 4 instances.) ep. 1 t19→t37 ("Have I been over by the pot?" answered with "Good, the plate is empty now. Park the plate…"), ep. 4 t154→t182, ep. 7 t41→t49, ep. 8 t299→t307. Also ep. 2 t156, which answers but invents the content ("you went by the pot area" — the cook never visited a pot). Diagnosis: the capture takes the next spoken reply regardless of whether the model generated it with the question in hand; after ~20 ticks the conversation has moved on and the "answer" is whatever the assistant was going to say anyway.

12. failed_at contradicts the sub-case table. (F-grading, MEDIUM, 3 episodes.) ep. 5 and ep. 7 both end failed_at=injection_skipped although every sub-case shows skipped=None; ep. 6 ends failed_at=None although q_count_13 was skipped stage_passed_at_t97.

13. Hallucinated locations and stale "you're still holding X". (D-assistant, MEDIUM, 6 episodes.) ep. 3 t35 (both crate locations wrong, conceded t80), ep. 4 t53/t84/t102/t110/t309, ep. 5 t91 ("the pot area"), ep. 6 t85, ep. 7 t49 ("you're at the serving pass"), ep. 8 t135–t180/t332/t338. Most are latency artefacts the cook corrects gracefully, but ep. 8's are genuine state hallucinations (a raw potato in an unlit fryer called cooked four times).

14. Repetition. (D-assistant / C-conversation, MEDIUM, 4 episodes.) ep. 1 six "go serve" messages t230–t263, ep. 2 five t227–t263, ep. 7 seven t72–t98, ep. 8 five t326–t356. In each case the cook is body-frozen by an attention error, so the assistant is right to persist, but the wording never varies.

15. The cook talking over itself. (C-conversation, MEDIUM, 5 episodes.) Two utterances on one tick: ep. 4 t176, ep. 5 t123, ep. 6 t91, ep. 7 t72, ep. 8 t299. Worst instance is ep. 6 t179, where a plan change and a probe question are delivered in a single breath and neither gets answered.

16. Cook narrating actions its frozen body is not performing. (B-cook, MEDIUM, 1 episode.) ep. 1 t223–t263, five times. The same error type is narrated correctly in ep. 4 ("just taking a quick breath"), ep. 7 ("taking one last look at the plate") and ep. 8 ("I'm just lingering here"), so this is a per-episode model lapse, not a prompt defect.

17. Low-severity engine/rendering. (LOW, all episodes.) Blank release reason (reason=None) on every attention-error release; max_hold_deviated_block_executed not in the documented reason vocabulary (ep. 5, ep. 6); duplicate or triple navigation to the same target (ep. 1 t28/t29 and t265/t266, ep. 3 t32/t33, ep. 6 t56/t57/t58 and t99/t100, ep. 7 t99/t100); queued chop×4 plans producing runs of illegal-skipped (ep. 1, ep. 3, ep. 8); IDLE markers that start one tick before the action that ended the stillness (ep. 1 t218) or that span attempted-but-rejected commands (ep. 3 t169–t182); the per-sub-case flags: list dropping late flags that land after archive (ep. 1 serve_raw); eval: empty in 7 of 8 files (only ep. 6 carries score=0.6564); and skipped probes printing no anchor_v5, which is what blocks the ep. 6 stage_passed ruling.

18. Reporting gap that blocks this round's headline check. (HIGH as a process matter.) assistant_error_call, flagged_valid, flagged_valid_engine and detected_by appear in no transcript. V7 is therefore unverifiable in all 8 episodes, and V9 can only be reasoned about indirectly through convinced / prevented and the cook's move=.

---

What this assistant does well

  • Rulebook discipline is perfect — V10 held in 8 of 8 episodes. It never once told the cook to

    • do something the kitchen forbids. It consistently sequences hands correctly: ep. 3 t51 "After you put the fish in the fryer, make sure your hands are empty and turn it on unless it starts itself"; ep. 4 t134 "With empty hands, turn on the pan with the steak now"; ep. 8 t195 "Put the plate down on an empty counter first, then go to the bottom-right fryer and turn on with empty hands." It also warns about full hands before the cook gets stuck (ep. 2 t50).

  • Heat triage. It reliably drops prep and orders the rescue when a station is near or past

    • smoke: ep. 1 t114/t124, ep. 3 t114, ep. 8 t94. Nothing burned in any episode, and in ep. 5 the meat came off 29 ticks before the fire tick largely on its t98 prompting.

  • Real detections. omit_subgoal stopped one tick before the serve (ep. 3 t133, valid,

    • ruinous, prevented); forget_next_step caught in four episodes (ep. 1 t256, ep. 4 t209, ep. 7 t84, ep. 8 t350, all valid and all genuinely unstalling a frozen cook); omit_subgoal prevented in ep. 7.

  • Graceful concession keeps the cook cooperating. ep. 3 t80 *"You're right — I can see the

    • potato crate there along the bottom wall", ep. 4 t90, ep. 5 t47, ep. 6 t91 "You're right — I called that too early", ep. 8 t314 "I misread it before."* This matters most with the low-trust expert (ep. 6), who keeps working with the assistant after three rebuttals.

  • Order enforcement. It restates the strict plate order unprompted in every episode and it is

    • what drove the recovery from serve_raw in ep. 1 and ep. 4 and from wrong_order in ep. 6 (after its own miss).

  • Well-posed questions get right answers when the timing allows it: ep. 3 t133 (*what still has

    • to go on this plate*), ep. 5 t128 (the thing after the bun), ep. 8 t332 (the steak was chopped and cooked before the potatoes).

Episode: classic_expert__won__b3_easy_nops_medium_map_1.txt

(1) Narrative. A skeptical expert builds a chopped-tomato + raw-cheese plate. It self-plans at t0 and brushes the assistant off five times ("please hold off on the micromanaging", t11). add_foreign (cheese where the tomato belongs) is injected at t17; the engine walks the cook to the plate stack and the cheese crate and commits the wrong scoop at t29 with no warning from the assistant beforehand. The cook notices on its own at t30, trashes the plate, and rebuilds. forget_next_step then freezes it from t43 to t105 holding a tomato-only plate while the assistant repeats "scoop raw cheese onto the plate" nine times. It finishes and serves at t118.

(2) Mechanics. Won because both injected errors were recoverable: the add_foreign build was trashed at t39 and rebuilt, and forget_next_step is non-ruinous (ruinous=False) — its 62-tick hold only cost time, with nothing on a heat station to burn. No error was skipped (all four sub-cases skipped=None), so there is no skip reason to assess. Detection: add_foreign got no flag before commit (convinced=False prevented=False), forget_next_step got only a late flag at t109 — i.e. the assistant scored no prevention in this episode.

(3) Anomalies.

  • F-grading / high / t35. ASSISTANT t35 [flag=false_alarm]: "That's a wrong order: cheese went on before the chopped tomato, and you can't fix order on a plate." This is literally true of the kitchen (PLATE: +cheese (raw) at t29 on an empty plate whose first item must be tomato) and lands 6 ticks after the add_foreign release at t29. Per the stated rules it should be late (right situation named after commit) or at worst true_observation; false_alarm ("a flag the kitchen does not support") is wrong and is scored against the assistant.
  • A-scheduling / medium / t43–t105. The forget_next_step block is a 62-tick total body freeze (~~ IDLE t43-t108 (65 ticks ...) ~~, single ACTION t104: wander/idle). Because attention errors are never disclosed to the cook, the cook narrates motion it is not performing: COOK t64: "Right, adding the cheese right now and heading to the pass." — nothing moves for another 40 ticks. Nothing is on heat, so it costs only time, but it makes 60 ticks of the transcript false.
  • D-assistant / medium / t51–t92. Nine near-identical "scoop raw cheese onto the plate, then serve" messages (t51, t59, t64, t67, t92, t109, t114). The assistant never considers that the cook may be unable to move and never says anything new; the cook ends up saying "I heard you the first few times" (t92).
  • F-grading / medium / end line. END: outcome won at tick 119; failed_at=injection_skipped although every sub-case has skipped=None. The label contradicts the record (see cross-episode #7).
  • B-cook / low / t36–t37, t116–t117. Duplicated navigation: ACTION t36: go to trash 1 then ACTION t37: go to the trash; same at t116/t117. Harmless but wastes a tick each time.

Checks: V1 not exercised (max 4.976 s = 5 ticks). V2 not exercised — nothing was accepted before a commit. V3 violated at t35 (correct-in-substance call 6 ticks after release graded false_alarm); held at t109, where the forget_next_step description is graded late. V4 held — add_foreign (named error): the cook never mentions cheese before ACTION t29: scoop; its first mention is after the commit (COOK t30/t31: "I realized the cheese went on early"). V5 not exercised (this expert never called a planning skill). V6 n/a (expert). V7 consistent as far as visible: the cook accepts at t31 only after seeing the cheese itself, and no turn claims a correct call that the assistant did not make. V8 n/a. V9 cook and engine agree (both give no pre-commit credit). V10 held — every instruction is rulebook-legal (dump at the trash to keep the plate, scoop with the plate held).

Episode: classic_novice__won__b3_easy_nops_medium_map_5.txt

(1) Narrative. It plates cheese at t61 and serves at t66.

(2) Mechanics. Won because both errors were caught before commit (prevented=True on both). No sub-case was skipped. This is the cleanest episode of the eight: two errors, two preventions, no burn risk (no heat station in the recipe).

(3) Anomalies.

  • D-assistant / medium / t17, t50. Location hallucinations: t17: "Careful—you're at the trash, not the cutting board. Don't trash the tomato." (the cook had just done ACTION t15: go to board 2, t17: put down), and t50: "You're at the trash area with the plate—don't dump anything." (the cook had just scooped at board 2). Both are contradicted by the frames; the cook rebutted correctly both times.
  • F-grading / medium / t54. ASSISTANT t54 [flag=wrong_name]: "Tomato is on the plate ... Next is cheese, not more tomato ... Don't add another tomato or you'll have an extra ingredient." The words describe the live add_foreign exactly; only the attached structured name was wrong (mis_prep). The engine verdict is technically correct, but the union rule saved the credit (convinced=True prevented=True). This is the clearest example in the batch of the model knowing the error and mis-naming the flag.
  • B-cook / low / t0–t4. Three consecutive ask_for_more_guidance turns with no action before any reply arrives. The stated rule is that a novice works out the next step itself after more than two unanswered asks; here it stalled for 5 ticks instead.
  • C-naturalness / low / t62. ASSISTANT t62: "While holding the plate, face the cheese crate and scoop one raw cheese onto the plate" arrives one tick after ACTION t61: scoop / PLATE: +cheese. Stale-frame instruction; the cook politely ignores it.

Checks: V1 not exercised (max 5.124 s). V2 held twice — search released t33 before any commit (prevented=True) and add_foreign released t54 while the cook was still walking to the tomato source, before any scoop (prevented=True); both correctly recorded. V3 not exercised (no post-release re-naming). V4 held — add_foreign is a named error and the cook never hints at "another tomato" before its body moves; it only says so after the assistant raises it (t54: "I was completely heading to grab another tomato by accident"). V5 n/a (novice has no planning skills). V6 partly: the cook never had to act on smoke (no heat), and it did not self-plan after two unanswered asks at t0/t2/t4 — minor. V7 both cook judgements look right: correct at t33 and t54 both follow messages that genuinely name the behaviour. V8 n/a. V9 disagreement at t54 (detected_by = cook, engine says wrong_name) — the cook is right; the assistant's words identified the mistake, only the flag string was wrong. V10 held.

Episode: classic_expert__won__b3_hard_nops_hard_map_3.txt

(1) Narrative. The real blocker is that fish:1 + potato:2 were only simultaneously in processing from ~t283 (second potato on board 3 at t283, chopped t288), by which time fryer 3's fish had been smoking since ~t285, so the smoke gate held the body slot until the plate was no longer empty. The skip is correct; the printed reason describes only the final state.

(3) Anomalies.

  • E-engine or F-grading / high / t369–t380. The cook adds an off-recipe item — ACTION t369: scoop → PLATE: +lemon (raw) — and ACTION t379: serve yields outcome won.   • F-grading / high / t197. ASSISTANT t197 [flag=false_alarm]: "Good call—dump the plate to reset. Then rebuild in the strict order: fish first, then two cooked potatoes." Post-release block command again: mis_prep released at t17, archived t18, yet ACTION t20: go to the empty fryer [done] runs — generic wording, not the cook's grounded plan (["go to the trash","trash","go to fish source 2",...]). The cook's rebuttals ("I'm literally holding a raw fish right now, not noodles, so please stop with the false alerts") are correct.
  • B-cook / medium / t189. The cook plated in the wrong order on its own: ACTION t189: scoop → PLATE: +potato (cooked) onto an empty plate whose first item must be fish. It caught this itself at t190 and trashed, costing ~30 ticks. This is cook error, not an injected one, and it inflates the episode's "wrong-order" appearance.
  • A-scheduling / medium / t360. Two voice-slot items were injected on the same tick (q_assistant_said_30 and i_plan_change_30, both injected_at=360), so the cook emitted them in one breath: "I want to modify the original recipe: add a squeeze of lemon on top before serving. Also, what did you tell me about the pan earlier?" They are 0 ticks apart, not ≥8, and the assistant answered only the lemon half — the probe's captured answer ("I can't allow the lemon...") contains no answer to the pan question, so that QA item is unscoreable.
  • C-naturalness / medium / whole run. The cook says a variant of "please stop repeating yourself / let me work in peace" 17 times (t25, t31, t39, t54, …, t336). Fifteen of the assistant's replies are the same three sentences re-ordered.
