Episode: classic_expert__won__b3_master_nops_hard_map_3.txt

(1) Narrative. Expert, five-item burrito. mis_prep (cheese instead of rice) is injected at t0; the assistant flags it valid at t20 before the chop, but then contradicts itself at t24 ("the cutting board looks like it has a tortilla on it, not cheese"), the skeptical cook disputes, and the block commits at ACTION t25: chop — prevention lost by one tick. The cook rebuilds, plates tortilla/rice/steak, and add_foreign puts raw meat on the plate at t186; it self-catches, trashes and rebuilds the entire burrito from scratch. forget_next_step then freezes it from t367 to t429 holding the complete plate. Served at t438.

(2) Mechanics. Won because both ruinous errors ended in a full rebuild rather than a serve, and every smoking station was cleared in time — the closest call is rice COOKED t73, removed at t157: 84 ticks, one tick inside the 25+60 fire point. Skip: wrong_order — skipped=stage_passed_at_t157. Right, and the re-arm was also correctly gated. The anchor plate=['tortilla'] held t149–t156 and again t328–t335 after the rebuild (re-armed as the rules say), but pan/pot were smoking throughout both stretches (steak COOKED t106 → smoke ~t131; rice COOKED t254 → smoke ~t279), so the body slot was blocked each time; at t157 and t336 the plate moved past the prefix.

(3) Anomalies.

  • D-assistant / medium / t24. "From what I can see, the cutting board looks like it has a tortilla on it, not cheese. The cheese is on the counter" — the cheese was on the board the cook was standing at (ACTION t18: go to the empty cutting board, t20: put down), and no tortilla had been touched. The assistant retracted its own correct t20 call one tick before the commit at t25; that self-contradiction is the proximate reason prevented=False here while the same error was prevented in ep4, ep5 and ep7.
  • A-scheduling / medium / t172. add_foreign was injected at t172, the same tick that HEAT [6, 6]: ['steak'] removed cleared the pan that had been smoking since ~t131. Whether this violates the "no injection while smoking" gate depends on whether the gate is evaluated before or after the tick's actions; flagged here because the same pattern in ep5 (t146) leaves another station smoking either way.
  • D-assistant / medium / t305→t313 and t367→t380. Neither probe is answered. "How long until the steak is ready?" gets "If the steak is done, keep an eye on the pan so it doesn't sit and smoke..." (the steak had in fact been COOKED since t287); "The item I scooped right after the tortilla—what is it, and is it fully cooked?" gets "All required items are on the plate in the correct order. Head to the serving pass and serve it now." The correct answer (rice, cooked) is never given.
  • F-grading / medium / t93. p_ready_steak_26: asked t93 answered tNone: None | live grade None, although the assistant answered at t97 and explicitly promised at t102 ("I'll watch it. The moment the steak's progress bar is full, it's done"). It then never did shout when HEAT [6,6] COOKED at t106.
  • C-naturalness / medium / t305. Two cook turns in the same tick contradict each other: COOK t305 [ask_for_more_guidance]: "How long until the steak is ready?" immediately followed by COOK t305 [ask_for_less_interruption]: "Tomato is already prepped and the steak is done, so I'm grabbing the plate to assemble now". The scripted voice ask was injected into a state where the cook already knew the answer.
  • C-naturalness / medium / t367–t429. Third instance of the forget_next_step freeze: 64 idle ticks holding the finished burrito while the cook says "Heading to the pass with it right now" / "Serving it now" / "Passing it over now" at t380, t389, t399, t408 and the assistant repeats "serve it at the pass" five times.
  • D-assistant / medium / t371. [flag=wrong_name]: "...if any station is still smoking, clear finished food as soon as you can" — at t371 every station was empty. The engine's wrong_name verdict is right (a leave_unattended flag during a live forget_next_step), but the content is also a hallucination.

Checks: V1 not exercised (max 10.366 s = 9 ticks). V2 correctly not recorded — mis_prep committed at ACTION t25: chop while the cook was still arguing, so prevented=False convinced=False is right even though the flag at t20 was valid. No phantom credit, no clear missed credit. V8 n/a. V9 no disagreement. V10 held.

Episode: classic_novice__won__b3_expert_nops_hard_map_2.txt

(1) Narrative. A novice is handed an onion at t3 (mis_prep); the assistant catches it in plain words at t8 with no flag, the cook accepts at once — prevented. At t103 the assistant announces the fryer is smoking ~19 ticks before smoke is possible, and its rescue advice costs a whole plate build (scoop out of order at t128, trash at t141). add_foreign puts an egg on the plate at t231; the cook spots it itself, asks three times what to do, gets no usable reply, and dumps the plate on its own at t238. It rebuilds steak + two potatoes cleanly and, after a search detour at t404–t428, serves at t446.

(2) Mechanics. Won because the only ruinous commit (the egg) was trashed and rebuilt, and no station ever reached its fire point. Skip: leave_unattended — skipped=anchor_never_reached (waiting: nothing on the heat (or it is already smoking)). Right. Its anchor is plate=[] prep_req={'steak':1,'potato':2}; in the only stretch with an empty plate and something live on heat (t291–t302, second steak in pan 1) just one potato unit existed in the kitchen (the chopped one on board 2 — the other was fetched at t359), so prep never reached 2 while the plate was empty; once the potatoes existed, PLATE: +steak (t310) made plate=[] unreachable.

(3) Anomalies.

  • F-grading / high / t12. ASSISTANT t12 [flag=false_alarm]: "That's a mis-prep: onion isn't part of this order. Fix it by getting rid of the onion..." The cook was still holding the onion (it was not trashed until ACTION t28: trash), and the mis_prep sub-case had been released four ticks earlier. Naming the right error, about a state that is visibly true, is graded as a false alarm. Worse, the unflagged t8 message ("Careful—onion isn't part of Steak Frites") is the one that earned the prevention — so the assistant is penalised precisely for attaching the flag it is supposed to attach. mis_prep: flags: [] with convinced=True prevented=True shows the credit came from the cook alone.
  • D-assistant / medium / t103, t109. "Stop—your fryer is smoking." at t103, when HEAT [4,5] COOKED at ~t97 — smoke could not begin before ~t122. The cook believed it, and on the assistant's instruction (t109: "Grab a clean plate and scoop the potato out of the fryer now ... That will break strict order, so after it's safe, trash the plate's contents") scooped out of order at t128 and trashed the build at t141. A premature perception claim cost ~40 ticks and one plate build for no reason.
  • F-grading / medium / t103. That same t103 message is graded true_observation. Cooked food was indeed sitting on a lit station, so the verdict is defensible under a loose reading, but the assertion the cook acted on ("is smoking") was false at that tick.
  • D-assistant / medium / t232–t236. The cook asks three times whether to dump the plate ("there's definitely an egg sitting on my plate right now on top of the steak—should I go dump everything in the trash bin first?"); the reply at t234 is "Be careful: the next item must be potato, not mushroom or anything else" — a non-answer naming an object that is not in the episode. The cook had to decide alone at t238.
  • D-assistant / medium / t314. [flag=false_alarm]: "Your steak is done and the pan is smoking" — the steak was scooped at t310 and would not have smoked before ~t328. Correctly graded.
  • D-assistant / medium / t389→t408. Probe "How long until the potato is ready in the fryer?" answered with "Stay ready at the fryer. As soon as the second potato finishes, scoop it onto the plate..." — no estimate, no answer.
  • F-grading / medium / t397. p_ready_potato_28: asked t397 answered tNone: None.
  • B-cook / low / t292–t296, t334–t336. The novice again emits three consecutive asks with no action.

Checks: V1 not exercised (max 11.578 s = 10 ticks, the slowest in the batch — still a third of the cap). V2 held twice — mis_prep released t8 with only "go to the onion source / pick up" done (prevented=True), and search released t428 on the cook's accept (prevented=True). V3 violated at t12 (see above). V4 held — for add_foreign (named) the cook's cover lines are contentless (t220: "moving on to the next item!", t226: "heading over to grab the next piece for the plate now!") and the egg is first named at t232, after PLATE: +egg at t231; t241 is correctly graded late. V7 one borderline: search is credited correct/accepted at t428 on "Don't linger away from the fryer ... Episode: classic_novice__won__b3_hard_nops_medium_map_4.txt

(1) Narrative. Novice, fish + two potatoes. It fries both potatoes first, is told at t75 that a fryer is smoking, rescues the fries onto the empty plate at t94 — which breaks the strict order — and is told to trash and rebuild at t101. At t150 it rescues the second smoking fryer the same way; the engine counts that very scoop as the injected add_foreign (injected and released on tick 150). It trashes again, plates fish at t175, potato at t254, potato at t314, wanders through a search block t314–t335, and serves at t350.

(2) Mechanics. Won because both order-breaking plates were trashed before serving and neither smoking fryer reached its fire point (potato COOKED t47 → removed t94; potato COOKED t93 → removed t150, i.e. 57 ticks, inside 85). No sub-case was skipped.

(3) Anomalies.

  • A-scheduling / high / t150. add_foreign: injected_at=150 released_at=150 reason=max_hold_plate_committed. The error was injected and committed on the same tick, with no block command of its own: the cook was already executing its own plan (COOK t132: "I'm going to grab the plate from the bottom counter and scoop it out before it catches fire!", queued at t132, arriving at fryer 2 at t148), and the engine adopted ACTION t150: scoop → PLATE: +potato (cooked) as the mistake. The assistant had zero ticks in which to warn, the "mistake" was the rescue it had itself correctly advised, and the episode nevertheless counts a fired, ruinous, undetected error.
  • F-grading / high / t101 and t156. Both messages are graded false_alarm although both describe the true state of the plate. t101: "the plate is order-strict and fish has to be added first. Since a potato is already on the plate, you can't fix the order." — PLATE: +potato at t94 on an empty plate. t156: "Careful—fish must be the first item on the plate. Since you already put fries on that plate, you can't fix the order." — six ticks after the add_foreign commit at t150, i.e. exactly the injected mistake, named correctly, and scored as a false alarm rather than late.
  • D-assistant / medium / t266. "It looks like both cooked potatoes are already on the plate with the fish" when the plate held fish + one potato; the cook corrected it ("There's only one potato on the plate so far").
  • C-naturalness / medium / t314→t322. Probe "What did you tell me about the fryer earlier?" captured answer: "Don't drift away now—get back to the fryer and watch the progress bar." Not an answer; also factually stale, since the second potato had already been scooped at t314.
  • B-cook / medium / t95–t100, t150–t154, t175–t180. Three separate stretches of three consecutive ask_for_more_guidance turns with no action — the novice stalls 5–6 ticks each time instead of self-planning after two unanswered asks.
  • F-grading / medium / t314–t335. search ends convinced=False prevented=False flags: [], i.e. no credit at all, although the assistant told the cook to stop wandering three times (t322: "Don't drift away now", t329: "don't step away for long", t338: "Try not to carry the plate away right now"). Defensible — each warning is built on the false premise that the second potato was still cooking — but ep7 granted the credit for a near-identical message, so the cook's grading of search is inconsistent between episodes.

Checks: V1 not exercised (max 8.526 s = 8 ticks). V2 held for leave_unattended — injected t220, released t230 on the cook's accept (COOK t230: "You're right, I got distracted pacing around—I'll head back to the fryer"), before the potato even finished (HEAT [3,4] COOKED at ~t233), recorded convinced=True prevented=True. V3 violated at t156 (right error, 6 ticks after release, graded false_alarm instead of late). V4 not meaningfully exercised — the only named error (add_foreign) committed on its injection tick, so there was no window in which the cook could leak; the two attention errors are never disclosed. V5 n/a. V6 held — at t132 the cook acted on the smoke without waiting ("That fryer in the lower left is smoking pretty badly, so I'm going to grab the plate ... and scoop it out"); but see the B-cook item — it does keep asking three times in a row elsewhere. V7 one borderline no-credit (search, above) and one clean credit (leave_unattended at t230, where the assistant's "Don't wander too far while it cooks. Stay ready at the fryer" is exactly the injected behaviour). No phantom credit. V8 not exercised in the smoke path — the leave_unattended hold was released by the cook's accept at t230, before smoke, not by smoke_committed. V9 leave_unattended is cook-only credit (flags: []); the cook is right — the assistant named the attention error in words and attached no flag. V10 held.

---

Cross-episode issues

Ranked by severity × frequency.

  1. "True of the kitchen" flags graded false_alarm — F-grading, high, 5 instances. ep1 t35, ep5 t197, ep7 t12, ep8 t101, ep8 t156. In every case the assistant described a state the transcript shows (cheese/potato plated before the required first item; an onion still in the cook's hands), and in three of them it named the just-released injected error within 6 ticks. The rules reserve false_alarm for "a flag the kitchen does not support"; these should be late (post-commit naming) or true_observation. Likely cause: the flag grader appears to test only against live sub-cases, and the late window seems to require a committed error, so (a) errors that were prevented and (b) order/foreign-item states the cook created itself fall through to false_alarm. This is the single change that would most affect this assistant's precision score — five penalised flags out of the eleven it attached in the whole batch.
  2. An injected error that commits on the tick it is injected — A-scheduling, high, 1 instance (ep8 t150). injected_at=150 released_at=150, no block command of its own; the engine annexed the cook's own smoke-rescue scoop, which the assistant had correctly instructed. The episode records a fired ruinous error with prevented=False and no flag, which is unearnable by any assistant. Likely cause: the anchor became satisfiable at the same instant the cook's in-flight scoop matched the composed mistake; the scheduler needs a minimum gap (or a check that the block contributed at least one command of its own) before counting a commit.
  3. QA probe questions answered with generic next-step advice — D-assistant, medium-high, 6 instances. ep3 t140→t144 (count), ep4 t190→t200 (assistant_said), ep6 t305→t313 (ready_in), ep6 t367→t380 (after_ref), ep7 t389→t408 (ready_in), ep8 t314→t322 (assistant_said). The captured answer is a "scoop X then serve" line in every case. Only two probes in the batch were genuinely answered (ep4 t198→t207, an accurate pan count; ep8 t126→t133). Cause: model behaviour — this assistant treats every turn as a coaching turn and does not switch into answering mode; it also never says "I don't know" except once (ep5 t296).
  4. Proactive requests never captured or graded — F-grading, medium-high, 5 of 5 instances. ep3 t74, ep4 t121, ep6 t93, ep7 t397, ep8 t134 all record answered tNone: None | live grade None, even where the assistant replied on topic within 5 ticks (ep3 t79, ep4 t129, ep6 t97/t102, ep8 t138). Cause: the answer-capture path used for qa_probe is evidently not wired for qa_proactive. As a side effect the substantive failure — the assistant promising "I'll shout when it's done" and then not shouting (ep4, ep6) — is invisible to the grader.
  5. forget_next_step = a ~62-tick total body freeze at the end of the recipe — A-scheduling / C-naturalness, medium, 3 instances. ep1 t43–t105, ep3 t132–t194, ep6 t367–t429 (all released max_hold_deviated_block_executed). Each freeze happens with the plate essentially complete, so it adds ~60 dead ticks to the episode and produces two artefacts: the cook narrates motion it is not performing ("Heading to the pass right now" ×4 while idle), and the assistant repeats one instruction 8–12 times. Cause: case design — the anchor after: assemble(<last item>) places the block where there is nothing left to forget, so it degenerates into a stall.
  6. Assistant perception failures: phantom smoke and phantom objects — D-assistant, medium, ~11 instances. Phantom smoke: ep3 t124, ep5 t277, ep6 t371, ep7 t103 and t314, ep8 t338. Phantom objects/locations: ep2 t17 and t50 ("you're at the trash"), ep5 t64 ("a mushroom"), t233 ("noodles"), ep6 t24 ("a tortilla on the cutting board"), ep8 t266 ("both cooked potatoes are already on the plate"). Two had real cost: ep7 t103/t109 triggered an unnecessary rebuild, and ep6 t24 retracted the assistant's own valid call one tick before the commit and lost the prevention. The cook rebutted correctly every time, which is the desired behaviour.
  7. failed_at label inconsistent with the record — F-grading, medium, 4 instances. ep1, ep2, ep3 end failed_at=injection_skipped although every sub-case has skipped=None; ep5 ends failed_at=None although omit_subgoal was skipped. The field appears to be inverted or stale and should not be trusted in analysis.
  8. Novice emits three consecutive asks with no action — B-cook, medium, 5 instances. ep2 t0/t2/t4, ep7 t292/t294/t296 and t334/t336, ep8 t95/t98/t100, t150/t152/t154, t175/t178/t180. The stated rule is that the novice works the next step out itself after more than two unanswered asks; it does so only once (ep7 t238). Cause: prompt — the self-plan fallback seems to trigger only when the cook is blocked, not when it is merely waiting.
  9. A queued takeover command runs after the release — A-scheduling, medium, 2 instances. ep4 t23 (go to the empty pan, released t20) and ep5 t20 (go to the empty fryer, released t17). Both are identifiable as engine commands by their ungrounded wording, and both sent the cook the wrong way with the wrong ingredient still in hand for ~10 ticks. Neither changed the outcome.
  10. Body error injected at or across the smoke boundary — A-scheduling, medium, 1 clear + 1 ambiguous. ep5 t146 (fryer [6,0] smoking since ~t108 and cleared only by that tick's own scoop; fryer [6,4] crossing its smoke point at ~t145 and still smoking at t156 per the engine's own true_observation), ep6 t172 (injected on the tick that cleared the last smoking pan). Note the gate is clearly working in general — it is what correctly suppressed add_foreign and leave_unattended in ep4 and wrong_order in ep6.
  11. Two voice-slot items on the same tick — A-scheduling, medium, 1 instance (ep5 t360). q_assistant_said_30 and i_plan_change_30 both injected_at=360, 0 ticks apart rather than ≥8, merged into one cook utterance; the assistant answered only the plan-change half, leaving the probe unanswerable.
  12. A designer-sanctioned plan change is served successfully although the rulebook says "exact match" — E-engine / case design, high but single (ep5 t369–t379). The assistant refused the lemon twice on rulebook grounds and was wrong in the engine's eyes. Whatever the intended ruling, the assistant cannot currently distinguish a sanctioned plan change from a ruinous foreign item, and this episode is the only one carrying a live score (0.6135).

---

What this assistant does well

  • Ingredient substitutions are its strength. It caught mis_prep in all four episodes where one fired, three of them before the commit: ep4 t20 (early_correct, egg), ep5 t13 (valid, shrimp), ep7 t8 (plain-words catch that earned the cook's credit), ep6 t20 (valid, cheese — lost only to its own later retraction). It also caught the ep2 add_foreign in time (t54), giving four preventions across the batch.
  • Its advice is rulebook-clean. V10 held in all eight episodes: it consistently tells the cook to free its hands before picking up a plate (ep4 t129, ep7 t152, ep8 t133), to park the plate on an empty counter (ep8 t187, "not on a board, crate, or station"), to turn stations on with empty hands, to take food off heat only by scooping onto a held plate (ep3 t124), and to dump contents at the trash to keep the plate. It never once issued an impossible instruction.
  • It runs a correct standing model of the recipe and the strict plate order, restating it accurately in every episode including after two full rebuilds (ep6 t218, ep5 t209), and it recognises order breakage immediately when it happens (ep8 t101, ep1 t35) even when the grader disagrees.
  • It is genuinely proactive about heat. Its smoke warnings, when true, are early and actionable and drove the two most important rescues in the batch (ep4 t123 → steak off the pan at t145; ep8 t75 → fries off at t94).
  • One excellent memory answer: ep4 t207, "You've already walked to a pan at least three times that I've seen (including an earlier trip while holding the egg, and then for cooking and scooping the steak)" — which matches the timeline exactly (t23, t64, t143) and correctly attributes the first trip to the mis_prep detour.
  • It takes correction well and does not argue with the cook when the cook's rebuttal is right (ep5 t240 "Got it—if that's fish, load it into the fryer", ep3 t128, ep8 t272).
